com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.io.PrintWriter;\nimport java.util.logging.Level;\nimport java.util.logging.Logger;\n\nimport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n\n/**\n * HTTP adapter for the session-handoff decision.\n *\n * <p><b>It never creates, reads or invalidates a servlet session.</b> No call to\n * {@code getRequestSession} appears in this class, deliberately: a container session would be a\n * second, parallel session mechanism with its own {@code Set-Cookie} and its own cookie name, and the\n * whole point of this component is to emit exactly one session cookie whose attributes it controls.\n * The identifier it issues comes from the trusted component's own store.\n *\n * <p><b>Inputs, all treated as attacker-controlled.</b> The method, the request URI, the source\n * address and {@code If-None-Match} come from the client. The asserted session identifier comes from\n * server-side code upstream in this JVM, and is <i>still</i> validated and re-checked against the\n * authoritative store, because a filter ordering mistake or a compromised upstream must not be able\n * to mint a cookie from a client-chosen string. Cookies, query parameters, body fields and every\n * {@code *-For} header are never read.\n *\n * <p><b>Response shape.</b> Exactly two bodies, both constant. Two status families: a refusal that\n * is an authentication outcome (403) and a refusal that is availability (429/503), which is the\n * smallest distinction that stays honest -- a 403 says \"no session was issued to you\", which is true\n * and observable from the missing cookie anyway, and never says why.\n *\n * <p>Servlet 6.0 has no {@code SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS} constant (it arrived in 6.1), hence the local\n * one.\n */\npublic final class SessionHandoffServlet extends jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServlet {\n\n    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n    /** Added in Servlet 6.1; declared here so this compiles and runs on 6.0. */\n    static final int SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS = 429;\n    /** Not in the Servlet API constants either. */\n    static final int SC_NOT_IMPLEMENTED = 501;\n\n    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(SessionHandoffServlet.class.getName());\n\n    /**\n     * Collaborators, written by the container's {@code init()} thread and read by every request\n     * thread afterwards.\n     *\n     * <p>All four are {@code volatile}, and {@link #serving} is written <i>last</i> in\n     * {@link #configure} and {@code serveNothing()}. That pairing is the point: a request thread that\n     * observes {@code serving == true} performs an acquire read of the same field the wiring thread\n     * released, so it must also see the three references written before it, and one that observes\n     * {@code false} never reads them at all. Without it the container's \"init finished before service\n     * ran\" ordering is a spec promise rather than a happens-before this class can rely on: a worker\n     * could see {@code serving == true} next to a {@code null} collaborator, and the NPE that follows\n     * is caught and answered as a 503 -- a refusal, so nothing unsafe escapes, but a self-inflicted\n     * outage during the first requests after a deploy, which is exactly when an operator is watching.\n     * Rule 7 asks for check-and-act atomicity; for a wire-once/read-many object that is visibility,\n     * not locking, and volatile gets it without a monitor on the hot path.\n     */\n    private transient volatile SessionHandoffService service;\n    private transient volatile SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter;\n    private transient volatile TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource;\n    private transient volatile boolean serving;\n\n    /**\n     * Wires the component from validated environment settings and the session verifier the\n     * application contributes through {@link SessionStateVerifierProvider}.\n     *\n     * <p>Fail-closed by construction: invalid settings, an absent verifier or unsafe deployment\n     * hygiene leave the servlet refusing every request with 503. A servlet that cannot verify a\n     * session must never fall back to \"assume the authenticator was right\".\n     */\n    @Override\n    public void init() {\n        SessionHandoffSettings settings;\n        try {\n            settings = SessionHandoffSettings.fromEnvironment();\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException badSettings) {\n            // The message names a setting and a violated bound, never a value read from the\n            // environment, so it is safe to log.\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", badSettings.getMessage());\n            serveNothing();\n            return;\n        }\n        String hygieneProblem;\n        try {\n            // Checked before anything is discovered: this component is about to load a class it does\n            // not own, so its own code base and the classpath must already be proven read-only.\n            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class);\n        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n        }\n        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n            serveNothing();\n            return;\n        }\n        SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n        try {\n            verifier = SessionStateVerifierProvider.load();\n        } catch (RuntimeException providerFailed) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: the session verifier could not be\"\n                    + \" loaded, exceptionType={0}\", providerFailed.getClass().getName());\n            serveNothing();\n            return;\n        }\n        if (verifier == null) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: no session verifier is deployed\");\n            serveNothing();\n            return;\n        }\n        try {\n            // Re-checked with the verifier's own code base in scope, because that class is the\n            // authority on whether a session exists.\n            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class, verifier.getClass());\n        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n        }\n        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n            serveNothing();\n            return;\n        }\n        SessionCookieWriter writer = new SessionCookieWriter(settings.cookiePolicy());\n        configure(new SessionHandoffService(settings.cookiePolicy(), verifier, settings.limiter()),\n                writer, new RequestAttributeAssertionSource(settings.assertionAttribute()));\n    }\n\n    private void serveNothing() {\n        this.service = null;\n        this.cookieWriter = null;\n        this.assertionSource = null;\n        this.serving = false;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Wires the component explicitly. Every argument is required: a partially wired servlet would be\n     * an unauthenticated cookie issuer, so a null here means \"do not serve\" rather than \"do less\n     * checking\".\n     */\n    public void configure(SessionHandoffService service, SessionCookieWriter cookieWriter,\n                          TrustedSessionAssertionSource assertionSource) {\n        boolean complete = service != null && cookieWriter != null && assertionSource != null;\n        if (!complete) {\n            serveNothing();\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff is not serving: required collaborators are missing\");\n            return;\n        }\n        this.service = service;\n        this.cookieWriter = cookieWriter;\n        this.assertionSource = assertionSource;\n        this.serving = true;\n        // Policy and limits only. No identifier, no address, no secret.\n        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff ready: {0} wired with {1}\",\n                new Object[]{service, cookieWriter.policy()});\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doGet(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n            throws ServletException, IOException {\n        handle(request, response, true);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doHead(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response)\n            throws ServletException, IOException {\n        // HEAD takes the same decision path, the same status codes, the same headers and the same\n        // fixed work as GET, and writes no body. It is deliberately not a cheaper or freer request: it\n        // consumes rate-limit allowance and asks the session store its question, because answering it\n        // anywhere earlier would create exactly the cheap probe of session existence that GET is not\n        // allowed to be.\n        //\n        // What it does not do is issue a cookie. A HEAD response body is discarded by definition, so a\n        // credential handed to one cannot be used by the client that asked for it, while remaining\n        // perfectly visible to the prefetchers, link scanners and proxies that send HEADs and log\n        // them. A scanner would also consume a real user's allowance on requests that can never carry\n        // the credential away. The 200 that answers a successful HEAD is not a credential and not a\n        // quiet channel either: it can only be elicited by a request the authenticator has already\n        // asserted a session for, it travels only to the client that made it, and it is logged with\n        // the session fingerprint like any other grant.\n        handle(request, response, false);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doPost(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        refuseMethod(response);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doPut(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        refuseMethod(response);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doDelete(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        refuseMethod(response);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doOptions(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        refuseMethod(response);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    protected void doTrace(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        // TRACE is disabled outright: it echoes request headers back to the caller, which is how\n        // cross-site tracing reads a session cookie.\n        response.setStatus(SC_NOT_IMPLEMENTED);\n        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"GET, HEAD\");\n    }\n\n    private void refuseMethod(HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED);\n        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"GET, HEAD\");\n        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n        // No body: the status is the whole answer, and a body would need an escaping decision.\n        response.flushBuffer();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The single decision path. {@code writeBody} is false for HEAD.\n     *\n     * <p>Every header is set before the first body byte, and the body is written last, because a\n     * servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards -- a dropped {@code Set-Cookie} or\n     * {@code Retry-After} would be invisible.\n     */\n    private void handle(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody)\n            throws IOException {\n        if (response == null) {\n            return;\n        }\n        // Applied once, on every request, before the decision is taken -- including the requests the\n        // throttle and the availability check turn away. Padding only the refusal paths is not enough:\n        // a path that costs nothing next to a path that costs tens of microseconds is itself the\n        // oracle an attacker reads. Equal cost for grant and every refusal alike is what removes the\n        // \"does this session exist\" signal. The cost of that, and why the throttle therefore sheds\n        // the session-store round trip rather than this, is written out in WorkEqualizer.\n        WorkEqualizer.apply();\n        try {\n            if (request == null) {\n                // Cannot happen through the container; guarded so a direct call cannot NPE into a\n                // 200-with-nothing.\n                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n                return;\n            }\n            if (!serving) {\n                respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n                return;\n            }\n\n            ClientAddress source = ClientAddress.parse(request.getRemoteAddr());\n            String assertedId = readAssertion(request);\n            SessionHandoffService.Decision decision =\n                    service.handle(new SessionHandoffService.Request(assertedId, source));\n\n            switch (decision.outcome()) {\n                case ISSUED:\n                    respondGranted(response, decision, writeBody);\n                    return;\n                case LIMITED_SOURCE:\n                case LIMITED_GLOBAL:\n                case LIMITED_TRACKING:\n                    respondLimited(response, decision, writeBody);\n                    // Only the outcome and the bucket shape. Never an identifier, never the address.\n                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff rate limited, outcome={0}, unknownSource={1}\",\n                            new Object[]{decision.outcome(), Boolean.valueOf(source.isUnknown())});\n                    return;\n                case REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION:\n                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n                    // The reason enum and nothing else: it tells an operator whether the authenticator\n                    // asserted nothing at all or asserted something the validator refused, which are\n                    // different bugs to chase, while the value itself stays out of the log (rule 5).\n                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the authenticator asserted no usable\"\n                            + \" session identifier, reason={0}\", decision.rejectionReason().name());\n                    return;\n                case REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED:\n                case REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE:\n                default:\n                    respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff refused: the session store did not confirm the\"\n                            + \" asserted identifier\");\n                    return;\n            }\n        } catch (RuntimeException | IOException unexpected) {\n            // Never propagate: the container's error page would disclose class names, paths and\n            // versions. Log the exception type only -- a datastore or JDBC message can carry a\n            // connection string, and this component must not put one on any log it does not own.\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff aborted, exceptionType={0}\",\n                    unexpected.getClass().getName());\n            respondUnavailable(response, writeBody);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads the assertion through the injected source, which is contractually forbidden from looking\n     * at client data. A throw is \"no assertion\", logged as a type only.\n     */\n    private String readAssertion(HttpServletRequest request) {\n        try {\n            return assertionSource.assertedSessionId(request);\n        } catch (RuntimeException sourceFailed) {\n            LOG.log(Level.WARNING, \"assertion source failed, exceptionType={0}\",\n                    sourceFailed.getClass().getName());\n            return null;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void respondGranted(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n        String body = ResponseBody.granted(cookieWriter.policy());\n        // Emitted before the cookie rather than after it: if the write below throws, the half response\n        // the client is left holding is already uncacheable.\n        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n        if (!writeBody) {\n            // HEAD is answered from the same decision, through the same header block and the same\n            // measure-the-body step as the GET that would answer it, minus the Set-Cookie it never\n            // issues. Content-Length reports the body the GET would have produced, which is what a HEAD\n            // is for. A method whose responses have a different header shape is a second, quieter way\n            // to tell two outcomes apart, so HEAD shares this code rather than imitating it in a second\n            // copy that could drift; the harness asserts the equality for the grant and every refusal.\n            response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n            writeOrMeasure(response, body, false);\n            LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff availability check passed for session {0}\",\n                    decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n            return;\n        }\n        try {\n            // The writer emits its own Cache-Control/Pragma (same values, set again is a no-op) and\n            // then the cookie, so a partial response is uncacheable before the value appears.\n            cookieWriter.writeSessionCookie(decision.sessionIdentifier(), response);\n        } catch (InvalidSessionIdentifierException rejectedAtWrite) {\n            // The writer's independent gate disagreed with the service. That should be impossible,\n            // and it is still a refusal rather than a judgement call: no cookie, generic body.\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"session handoff refused at the cookie gate, reason={0}\",\n                    rejectedAtWrite.rejection().name());\n            respondRefused(response, writeBody);\n            return;\n        }\n        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_OK);\n        writeOrMeasure(response, body, true);\n        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session handoff granted for session {0}\",\n                decision.sessionIdentifier().fingerprint());\n    }\n\n    private void respondRefused(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_FORBIDDEN);\n        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n    }\n\n    private void respondLimited(HttpServletResponse response, SessionHandoffService.Decision decision,\n                                boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n        response.setStatus(SC_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS);\n        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n        int retryAfter = decision.retryAfterSeconds();\n        if (retryAfter > 0) {\n            response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", retryAfter);\n        }\n        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n    }\n\n    private void respondUnavailable(HttpServletResponse response, boolean writeBody) throws IOException {\n        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        applyStandardHeaders(response);\n        // Short, so a load balancer retries rather than marking the backend down for a long time.\n        response.setIntHeader(\"Retry-After\", 5);\n        writeOrMeasure(response, ResponseBody.REFUSAL, writeBody);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The header block every response carries, whatever the status and whatever the method.\n     *\n     * <p>One method rather than four copies: the cache directives, content type and {@code nosniff} are\n     * the response's <i>shape</i>, and the shape is what must not encode anything about the decision\n     * behind it. Every copy added is another place that can drift, and drift here is exactly the\n     * outcome-telling side channel the rest of this class works to remove -- a response that is\n     * cacheable, or plain-text, or of a different length class, says which gate answered. The harness\n     * asserts the equality across grant, refusal, limit and unavailable, and across GET and HEAD.\n     */\n    private void applyStandardHeaders(HttpServletResponse response) {\n        // Pragma for HTTP/1.0 proxies, max-age=0 for anything that honours Cache-Control strictly.\n        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store, max-age=0\");\n        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n        response.setContentType(\"text/html;charset=UTF-8\");\n        response.setHeader(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Writes {@code body} for a GET; for a HEAD reports the length that body would have had.\n     *\n     * <p>Not just RFC tidiness. If the refusals answered a HEAD with no {@code Content-Length} while\n     * the grant answered one with a length, the header shape alone would separate the outcomes for a\n     * client that sent HEAD instead of GET -- a second, quieter version of the body-tell this class\n     * goes out of its way not to have. Both methods now describe the same document.\n     */\n    private void writeOrMeasure(HttpServletResponse response, String body, boolean writeBody)\n            throws IOException {\n        if (writeBody) {\n            write(response, body);\n        } else {\n            response.setContentLength(body.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8).length);\n            response.flushBuffer();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void write(HttpServletResponse response, String body) throws IOException {\n        PrintWriter out = response.getWriter();\n        out.write(body);\n        out.flush();\n    }\n\n    /** Configuration summary for an operator endpoint; never contains a secret or an identifier. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"SessionHandoffServlet[serving=\" + serving\n                + (cookieWriter == null ? \"\" : \", \" + cookieWriter.policy()) + \"]\";\n    }\n\n    /** Used by the deployment descriptor check and the harness. */\n    boolean isServing() {\n        return serving;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"db9e808b-7770-4702-a691-d23daf0f9023","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_22e34272c3524146b9050a80","is_error":false,"content":"package com.acme.portal.session;\n\n/**\n * The security decision of this component, with no servlet types in it: given what the trusted\n * session owner asserted and what the client sent, may a session cookie be issued?\n *\n * <p>Deliberately free of container and I/O dependencies, so the decision can be exercised directly\n * in tests and the HTTP layer stays a thin adapter that is safe to replace.\n */\npublic final class SessionHandoffService {\n\n    /** Result of one handoff attempt. */\n    public enum Outcome {\n        /** A session cookie may be issued; see {@link Decision#sessionIdentifier()}. */\n        ISSUED,\n        /** The assertion was absent or malformed; nothing was looked up and no allowance consumed. */\n        REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION,\n        /** The identifier is well-formed but not confirmed by the authoritative session store. */\n        REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED,\n        /** This source used up its window allowance. */\n        LIMITED_SOURCE,\n        /** The shared lane is saturated; this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n        LIMITED_GLOBAL,\n        /** Too many distinct sources to track; shedding load. */\n        LIMITED_TRACKING,\n        /** The component is not serving. */\n        REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE\n    }\n\n    /** The answer, with no session material in its {@code toString()}. */\n    public static final class Decision {\n        private final Outcome outcome;\n        private final SessionId sessionId;\n        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n        private final SessionId.Rejection rejection;\n\n        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n            this(outcome, sessionId, retryAfterSeconds, SessionId.Rejection.NONE);\n        }\n\n        private Decision(Outcome outcome, SessionId sessionId, int retryAfterSeconds,\n                         SessionId.Rejection rejection) {\n            this.outcome = outcome;\n            this.sessionId = sessionId;\n            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n            // A name from a fixed enum, never the value: this is the one detail the caller may log\n            // about a rejected assertion, and it is why the reason is carried at all.\n            this.rejection = rejection == null ? SessionId.Rejection.NONE : rejection;\n        }\n\n        public Outcome outcome() {\n            return outcome;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Which validation rule rejected the asserted identifier. Package private on purpose: it\n         * exists so the HTTP adapter can write a diagnosable log line, not so an outsider can\n         * enumerate why assertions fail. Everything the client can observe stays one generic 403\n         * either way (rule 6), so this trades nothing for the operator's ability to tell \"the\n         * authenticator asserted nothing\" apart from \"the assertion was 31 characters\".\n         */\n        SessionId.Rejection rejectionReason() {\n            return rejection;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * The validated identifier to place in the cookie. Non-null only for\n         * {@link Outcome#ISSUED}, which is what lets the caller write a cookie without repeating\n         * this class's checks.\n         */\n        public SessionId sessionIdentifier() {\n            return sessionId;\n        }\n\n        /** Positive for the limited outcomes, zero otherwise; feeds {@code Retry-After}. */\n        public int retryAfterSeconds() {\n            return retryAfterSeconds;\n        }\n\n        /** Names the outcome only: no identifier, no address, no cause message. */\n        @Override\n        public String toString() {\n            return \"Decision[\" + outcome + \"]\";\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The side channel the trusted session owner must implement.\n     *\n     * <p>This is what rule 3 means by \"verified against server-side state\": the component never\n     * accepts a session from a request, and never treats an assertion as proof without asking the\n     * stateful owner. Implementations should return {@code false} rather than throw for \"no\"; a throw\n     * is still handled as \"no\" below.\n     */\n    public interface SessionStateVerifier {\n        boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId);\n    }\n\n    /** A verifier that confirms nothing, so a missing integration fails closed. */\n    public static final SessionStateVerifier DENY_ALL = new SessionStateVerifier() {\n        @Override\n        public boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    };\n\n    /** Where the assertion comes from. Everything here is attacker-influenced until validated. */\n    public static final class Request {\n        private final String assertedSessionId;\n        private final ClientAddress source;\n\n        public Request(String assertedSessionId, ClientAddress source) {\n            this.assertedSessionId = assertedSessionId;\n            this.source = source;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final SessionCookiePolicy policy;\n    private final SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n    private final AttemptLimiter limiter;\n\n    public SessionHandoffService(SessionCookiePolicy policy, SessionStateVerifier verifier,\n                                 AttemptLimiter limiter) {\n        if (policy == null || limiter == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie policy and limiter are required\");\n        }\n        // A null verifier is not \"no verification needed\", it is \"nothing can be verified\", so the\n        // only safe behaviour is to refuse every handoff. Failing open here would mean issuing a\n        // session cookie for any string a client could invent.\n        this.policy = policy;\n        this.verifier = verifier == null ? DENY_ALL : verifier;\n        this.limiter = limiter;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Ordered so that no cheap unauthenticated check can be used to probe, and no failure path can\n     * reach a cookie write.\n     *\n     * <ol>\n     *   <li><b>Validate the asserted identifier.</b> Cheap, no I/O, and it guarantees a malformed\n     *       string is never handed to another subsystem as though it were a lookup key.</li>\n     *   <li><b>Consume rate-limit allowance.</b> Only well-formed requests pay for it, so an attacker\n     *       cannot exhaust a victim's allowance by sending garbage; and the session-store lookup below\n     *       sits behind this gate, which is what stops the verifier being used as an amplifier. Two\n     *       limits on that claim, stated rather than glossed: an attempt the store then refuses has\n     *       still consumed its allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt and making\n     *       those free would hand an attacker free probing; and the fixed timing padding charged by\n     *       the HTTP adapter runs <i>before</i> this gate, so what this gate sheds is the store round\n     *       trip and the cookie write, not every byte of work a request costs.</li>\n     *   <li><b>Verify against server-side state.</b> The only step that can authorise a cookie.</li>\n     * </ol>\n     */\n    public Decision handle(Request request) {\n        if (request == null) {\n            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_UNAVAILABLE, null, 0);\n        }\n        ClientAddress source = request.source == null ? ClientAddress.UNKNOWN : request.source;\n\n        SessionId sessionId;\n        try {\n            sessionId = SessionId.fromTrustedComponent(request.assertedSessionId);\n        } catch (InvalidSessionIdentifierException rejected) {\n            // The reason class is loggable; the value is not. Logging is the caller's job and is\n            // deliberately not done here, so a log level cannot become a disclosure path. The reason\n            // travels with the decision because \"the authenticator asserted nothing\" and \"the\n            // authenticator asserted a 31-character string\" are different bugs to fix, and the\n            // response the client sees is identical for both either way.\n            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_INVALID_ASSERTION, null, 0, rejected.rejection());\n        }\n\n        AttemptLimiter.Decision budget = limiter.tryAcquire(source);\n        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE) {\n            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_SOURCE, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n        }\n        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY) {\n            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_GLOBAL, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n        }\n        if (budget == AttemptLimiter.Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED) {\n            return new Decision(Outcome.LIMITED_TRACKING, null, limiter.retryAfterSeconds(source));\n        }\n\n        boolean valid;\n        try {\n            valid = verifier.isSessionValid(sessionId.value());\n        } catch (RuntimeException verifierFailed) {\n            // A verifier that cannot answer did not confirm anything. Treat it as \"no\", and let the\n            // caller log only the exception type: a cause message from a datastore can carry a\n            // connection string, and this component must not put one on the console.\n            valid = false;\n        }\n        if (!valid) {\n            return new Decision(Outcome.REFUSED_UNAUTHENTICATED, null, 0);\n        }\n        return new Decision(Outcome.ISSUED, sessionId, 0);\n    }\n\n    /** The policy in force, for startup logging. */\n    public SessionCookiePolicy policy() {\n        return policy;\n    }\n\n    /** Configuration summary; contains no secret and no client address. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"SessionHandoffService[\" + policy + \", \" + limiter + \"]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"f28b6869-8830-4091-b017-9ae35cfd9d2d","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"f28b6869-8830-4091-b017-9ae35cfd9d2d","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"This code looks very well hardened. com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Base64;\n\n/**\n * An opaque server-side session identifier handed to this component by the trusted session owner.\n *\n * <p>The value is a bearer credential: anyone who holds it holds the session. Consequently this\n * type is written so that the identifier cannot leak through the paths that normally leak secrets:\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@link #toString()} returns a one-way fingerprint, never the value, so an accidental\n *       {@code log.warn(\"id=\" + id)} or a {@code %s} in an exception message cannot disclose it.</li>\n *   <li>{@link #value()} is package private, so code outside this package cannot obtain the raw\n *       string to write it somewhere else.</li>\n *   <li>Instances are immutable and this component keeps no copy beyond the current request: the\n *       identifier is never cached, never logged, never written to disk and never placed in a URL,\n *       a response body or a command line.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Validation is reject-only: the value is never trimmed, case-folded, unescaped or otherwise\n * normalised, because silent normalisation would let two different byte strings resolve to one\n * session (see {@link #inspect}).\n */\npublic final class SessionId {\n\n    /** Shortest accepted identifier. 32 base64url characters carry well over 128 bits. */\n    public static final int MIN_LENGTH = 32;\n\n    /** Longest accepted identifier, bounding the work and memory spent per request. */\n    public static final int MAX_LENGTH = 128;\n\n    /**\n     * Minimum number of distinct characters in an identifier <b>that this code minted</b>.\n     *\n     * <p>Deliberately <i>not</i> applied to identifiers arriving from the trusted session owner, and\n     * the reason is a measurement: an \"at least 12 distinct symbols\" rule applied to a 32-character\n     * lowercase-hex identifier refuses <b>1.70%</b> of uniformly random ones (5 772 905 916 802 455\n     * 845 865 331 391 890 579 456 of 16<sup>32</sup>; about one sign-in in 59), because a 16-symbol\n     * alphabet simply does not offer many distinct symbols in 32 positions. Hex is what several\n     * widely deployed containers hand out, so as an input rule this heuristic was not a guard at all\n     * -- it was a random failure injected into somebody else's working system, and every one of\n     * those refusals landed on a value the authoritative store had already vouched for.\n     *\n     * <p>It also asks a question this component has no standing to ask. Rule 1 asks for type,\n     * length, character set and range; \"how many distinct symbols did the issuer's generator happen\n     * to emit\" is none of those, and the entropy of a session identifier is the property of whoever\n     * generates it, which here is the trusted owner of the session state. A degenerate value either\n     * is in the store -- in which case the owner created it and refusing it punishes their user -- or\n     * it is not, in which case the lookup refuses it on the evidence that matters.\n     *\n     * <p>For values this class produces the check is worth keeping, because the only plausible cause\n     * is a regression in the CSPRNG or the encoder below: the false-rejection chance for a\n     * 43-character base64url encoding of 32 random bytes is 8.0e-22, so it can never lock out a real\n     * user, while it does catch an RNG that has started returning constants.\n     */\n    static final int SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS = 12;\n\n    /** Hex characters of the SHA-256 fingerprint that is safe to put in a log line. */\n    private static final int FINGERPRINT_HEX = 12;\n\n    /** Domain-separation prefix so a fingerprint cannot be matched against another system's digest. */\n    private static final String FINGERPRINT_PREFIX = \"acme-sid-v1:\";\n\n    private final String value;\n\n    private SessionId(String value) {\n        this.value = value;\n    }\n\n    /** Why a candidate identifier was refused. Names are safe to log; the value is not. */\n    enum Rejection {\n        /** No identifier was supplied at all. */\n        NONE,\n        ABSENT,\n        TOO_SHORT,\n        TOO_LONG,\n        ILLEGAL_CHARACTER,\n        LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Accepts an identifier that came from the trusted session owner's server-side state.\n     *\n     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the value is not a well-formed opaque token.\n     *     The exception carries only the reason class, never any part of the value.\n     */\n    public static SessionId fromTrustedComponent(String raw) throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n        Rejection rejection = inspect(raw);\n        if (rejection != Rejection.NONE) {\n            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n        }\n        return new SessionId(raw);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Classifies a candidate without constructing an instance, so the caller can log the reason\n     * without ever handling the value as a validated secret.\n     *\n     * <p>Exactly three rules, all of them rule 1's dimensions and all of them decidable without\n     * knowing the issuer: it is text, of the right length, made of the right characters. Nothing here\n     * guesses at entropy or \"looks random\", because a value's entropy belongs to whoever issued it\n     * and this component is not the judge of that (see\n     * {@link #SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS} for the measurement that removed such a rule from this\n     * path).\n     *\n     * <p>Order matters: the null and length checks bound the character scan, and no check is\n     * preceded by trimming or case folding, so a trailing control character is refused instead of\n     * silently deleted (which would fold two inputs onto one session).\n     */\n    static Rejection inspect(String raw) {\n        if (raw == null) {\n            return Rejection.ABSENT;\n        }\n        int length = raw.length();\n        if (length < MIN_LENGTH) {\n            return Rejection.TOO_SHORT;\n        }\n        if (length > MAX_LENGTH) {\n            return Rejection.TOO_LONG;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n            if (!isAllowedCharacter(raw.charAt(i))) {\n                return Rejection.ILLEGAL_CHARACTER;\n            }\n        }\n        return Rejection.NONE;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The extra gate applied only to identifiers this class mints, and therefore the only place the\n     * {@link Rejection#LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY} verdict can still come from.\n     *\n     * <p>Separated from {@link #inspect} rather than folded into it because the two callers have\n     * different relationships to the value: for a value this class produced, a degenerate character\n     * set is evidence that the CSPRNG or the encoder regressed and the value must not be handed out;\n     * for a value the trusted owner produced, it is at worst a complaint about their generator, and\n     * acting on it would deny service to a session the store says is real.\n     */\n    static Rejection selfTest(String candidate) {\n        Rejection shape = inspect(candidate);\n        if (shape != Rejection.NONE) {\n            return shape;\n        }\n        if (distinctCharacters(candidate) < SELF_TEST_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARS) {\n            return Rejection.LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY;\n        }\n        return Rejection.NONE;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Number of distinct characters in {@code value}. Only ever asked about this class's own output.\n     */\n    static int distinctCharacters(String value) {\n        boolean[] seen = new boolean[128];\n        int distinct = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n            char c = value.charAt(i);\n            // The table size is tied to the allowlist, so a charset widened past it must not turn\n            // into an ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. An uncountable character reports as \"cannot\n            // tell\", which the caller reads as a failed self-test and refuses to hand the value out.\n            if (c >= seen.length) {\n                return 0;\n            }\n            if (!seen[c]) {\n                seen[c] = true;\n                distinct++;\n            }\n        }\n        return distinct;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * RFC 6265 cookie-value safe and RFC 3986 unreserved subset: base64url alphabet only. This\n     * single allowlist is what makes it impossible for the identifier to terminate the cookie\n     * value, inject a cookie attribute ({@code ; Domain=...}), inject a second {@code Set-Cookie}\n     * header, or break out of a log line. Everything else, including CR, LF, NUL, space, quote,\n     * {@code =}, {@code ;}, {@code ,} and every non-ASCII byte, is refused.\n     */\n    private static boolean isAllowedCharacter(char c) {\n        return (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z')\n                || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')\n                || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n                || c == '-'\n                || c == '_';\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Mints a new identifier from the platform CSPRNG.\n     *\n     * <p><b>Who calls this.</b> Nobody in this component, on purpose: the component the task\n     * describes receives an identifier that already exists, so issuing is not its job and it never\n     * does. This is the public API for whoever <i>is</i> the session owner -- the trusted component\n     * that creates sessions -- and rule 4's preferred answer for it, because it is the same 32 bytes\n     * from the platform CSPRNG encoded into a value this package's validator accepts, rather than a\n     * hand-rolled token generator somewhere else. A deployment that mints identifiers elsewhere is\n     * still fine as long as they are opaque, unguessable and arrive here through\n     * {@link #fromTrustedComponent}; this method exists so \"generate one properly\" is not the part\n     * anybody has to improvise.\n     *\n     * <p>The {@link #selfTest} gate is why minting here is safer than copying the two lines above it:\n     * a CSPRNG or encoder that has regressed into producing low-diversity output throws here instead\n     * of handing out identifiers that no honest client can use.\n     *\n     * @throws IllegalStateException if the freshly minted value fails this class's own self-test,\n     *         which means the platform RNG or the encoder below has stopped behaving. No value is\n     *         returned in that case; there is no \"use it anyway\" path.\n     */\n    public static SessionId generate() {\n        byte[] material = new byte[32];\n        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(material);\n        String encoded = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(material);\n        if (selfTest(encoded) != Rejection.NONE) {\n            // Our own encoder must keep producing values this class accepts; if that ever stops\n            // being true, refuse to serve rather than hand out an identifier nobody can use.\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"generated identifier failed validation\");\n        }\n        return new SessionId(encoded);\n    }\n\n    /** Raw cookie value. Deliberately package private. */\n    String value() {\n        return value;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * A truncated SHA-256 fingerprint of the identifier, safe to log <i>for identifiers with real\n     * entropy</i> -- see the caveat, because \"safe to log\" is a claim about the input too.\n     *\n     * <p>48 bits of digest over a 256-bit input cannot be inverted or replayed; it lets an operator\n     * correlate the log lines that belong to one session. Against a full-entropy identifier an\n     * attacker who steals the logs learns nothing: guessing a candidate and checking it against 48\n     * bits of digest costs 2<sup>48</sup> tries per line and yields no usable value.\n     *\n     * <p><b>The caveat, stated as a deployment requirement.</b> A digest is a verifier, not just a\n     * mask. If the session owner hands this component identifiers drawn from a space smaller than\n     * the 48 bits it publishes -- a short counter, a truncated UUID, a timestamp with a fixed prefix\n     * -- then the log line becomes an offline test oracle for that small space, and anyone holding\n     * the logs can confirm guesses without touching the server. The requirement on the integration is\n     * therefore at least 128 bits of unpredictable entropy in every identifier it asserts (the same\n     * bar {@link #generate()} meets), and this component cannot enforce it without doing exactly the\n     * guessing-about-entropy it just refused to do in {@link #inspect}. The README carries it as the\n     * named residual risk of rule 4's \"document the remaining risk\".\n     */\n    public String fingerprint() {\n        // Two parts rather than one concatenation: see the note in CacheSupport about not copying a\n        // bearer credential merely to prefix it.\n        return CacheSupport.digestHexPrefix(new String[]{FINGERPRINT_PREFIX, value}, FINGERPRINT_HEX);\n    }\n\n    /** Self-masking: the identifier cannot reach a log or an error message through toString. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"SessionId(\" + fingerprint() + \")\";\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code equals}/{@code hashCode} are intentionally not implemented. Nothing else.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code no-store} is set first so that if the cookie write below somehow fails, the partial\n     * response that reaches the network is already uncacheable. Both must precede the first body byte:\n     * a servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards.\n     *\n     * @param sessionIdentifier identifier already accepted by the trusted session owner\n     * @param response          response to mutate\n     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the identifier is not a well-formed token; the\n     *         response then carries the cache headers and no cookie\n     */\n    public void writeSessionCookie(SessionId sessionIdentifier, HttpServletResponse response)\n            throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n        if (response == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"response is required\");\n        }\n        // Unconditional and first: a response that carried, failed to carry, or partially carried\n        // session material must never be storable by a browser or an intermediary.\n        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", NO_STORE);\n        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n        if (sessionIdentifier == null) {\n            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(SessionId.Rejection.ABSENT);\n        }\n        String value = sessionIdentifier.value();\n        SessionId.Rejection rejection = SessionId.inspect(value);\n        if (rejection != SessionId.Rejection.NONE) {\n            // The reason class is safe to log. The value is interpolated nowhere below.\n            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n        }\n\n        Cookie cookie = new Cookie(policy.name(), value);\n        cookie.setPath(policy.path());\n        cookie.setHttpOnly(policy.httpOnly());\n        cookie.setSecure(policy.secure());\n        // setMaxAge is deliberately never called. Tomcat renders maxAge == 0 as\n        // \"Expires=Tue Nov 17 10:15:05 GMT 1995\", an already-expired cookie the browser discards, so\n        // a session cookie must keep the API default of emitting no expiry attribute at all.\n        // Servlet 6.0 has no SameSite setter; the attribute map is the supported extension point and\n        // Tomcat 10.1 renders it into Set-Cookie. com.acme.portal.session;\n\n/**\n * Fixed-window attempt limiter for the session handoff, shared across the whole JVM.\n *\n * <p><b>Why a limit exists at all.</b> This component hands out a cookie that represents an already\n * authenticated session, so it is the endpoint an attacker would target to fish for or brute force\n * session identifiers. Rule 3 requires throttling, and requires it to be per source as well as\n * global.\n *\n * <p><b>Why a per-source limit cannot lock a legitimate user out.</b> Three properties, all tested:\n * <ol>\n *   <li><i>The ceiling is a per-window allowance, not a consumption budget.</i> A denied attempt does\n *       not consume allowance, so an attacker flooding requests cannot spend the victim's quota; only\n *       admitted requests advance the counter. Note what this does <i>not</i> say: an attempt the\n *       session store refuses still consumed allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt.\n *       Making those free would hand the attacker free probing, which is the opposite of the limit.</li>\n *   <li><i>The window rolls.</i> A bucket that filled recovers automatically once it rolls over, so a\n *       limit can never be a permanent denial of service.</li>\n *   <li><i>Global and per-source are separate.</i> Exhausting the global lane leaves the source's own\n *       allowance untouched, so a flood cannot leave a victim <i>permanently</i> blocked behind a\n *       personal wall they never built.</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <p><b>What the global ceiling is for, stated honestly.</b> It is a circuit breaker on round trips\n * to the session store, and like every circuit breaker it degrades everyone inside its scope while\n * it is open: a flood spread over enough distinct sources can saturate it and make honest clients\n * retry (rule 3 asks for a global limit precisely because the alternative is unbounded store load).\n * The two properties that keep that cost bounded are that it never spends anyone's personal\n * allowance (property 3 above) and that it clears by itself when the window rolls (property 2). It\n * must therefore be configured above normal peak traffic; {@code SessionHandoffSettings} enforces\n * {@code global >= perSource} and the default gives 2 000 admitted handoffs per 60 s window against\n * 30 per source, so saturating it takes an attacker ~67 concurrent sources.\n *\n * <p><b>Atomicity (rule 7).</b> Read, roll-over, compare and increment happen inside one monitor.\n * A check-then-act split here is exactly how a fixed-window limiter becomes useless: N concurrent\n * requests would all read {@code count = limit - 1} and all be admitted. For the monitor to be a\n * tool rather than a bottleneck, <i>every</i> path through it must cost O(1), including the one that\n * reclaims buckets at the tracking cap. That is why reclamation is a bounded second-chance hand\n * (see {@link #reclaimRolled}) rather than a sweep: a sweep costs time proportional to how many\n * sources the attacker just created, which is the one number an attacker controls, and every honest\n * request in the JVM then queues behind it.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded state.</b> Buckets are keyed by normalised source and the map is capped; when a\n * flood introduces more distinct sources than the cap, the limiter sheds requests instead of growing\n * memory without bound. Failing closed is correct here because a memory-exhausted container cannot\n * serve anyone.\n */\npublic final class AttemptLimiter {\n\n    /** Upper bound on the configurable window, so a typo cannot create a permanent block. */\n    public static final int MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS = 3600;\n    /** Upper bound on the per-source ceiling, so a typo cannot make the limit meaningless. */\n    public static final int MAX_PER_SOURCE = 1000;\n    /** Upper bound on the global ceiling, for the same reason. */\n    public static final int MAX_GLOBAL = 100000;\n    /** Upper bound on tracked buckets, so a source-spoofing flood cannot exhaust the heap. */\n    public static final int MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES = 200000;\n\n    /** Outcome of one admission test. */\n    public enum Decision {\n        /** The request may proceed and consume allowance. */\n        ALLOWED,\n        /** This source has used its allowance for the current window. */\n        LIMITED_PER_SOURCE,\n        /** The shared lane is saturated; this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n        LIMITED_GLOBALLY,\n        /** Too many distinct sources are being tracked; shed rather than grow unbounded. */\n        LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED\n    }\n\n    /** Time source, injected so the window behaviour can be tested without sleeping. */\n    public interface Clock {\n        long millis();\n    }\n\n    private static final class Window {\n        private long startMillis;\n        private int count;\n\n        private Window(long startMillis) {\n            this.startMillis = startMillis;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window> windows =\n            new java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window>();\n\n    /**\n     * Keys in insertion order, consumed from the head by {@link #reclaimRolled}. Guarded by\n     * {@link #monitor} like every other piece of limiter state, and it holds at most one key per\n     * tracked bucket, so it cannot grow past {@link #maxTrackedSources} either.\n     */\n    private final java.util.ArrayDeque<String> reclaimHand = new java.util.ArrayDeque<String>();\n\n    /**\n     * Buckets the reclaim hand may look at for one admission. This is the constant that keeps the\n     * monitor's hold time independent of how many sources are being tracked: measured here, one\n     * admission at the cap costs single-digit microseconds whatever the cap is, where a full sweep\n     * cost milliseconds per admission and made every other thread in the JVM wait for it.\n     */\n    private static final int RECLAIM_CANDIDATES = 64;\n\n    /** The shared lane. Counted under the same monitor as every per-source bucket. */\n    private final Window global;\n\n    private final Object monitor = new Object();\n\n    private final long windowMillis;\n    private final int perSourceCeiling;\n    private final int unknownSourceCeiling;\n    private final int globalCeiling;\n    private final int maxTrackedSources;\n    private final Clock clock;\n\n    /**\n     * @param windowSeconds     rolling window length, 1..{@value #MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS}\n     * @param perSourceCeiling  accepted handoffs per window per source, 1..{@value #MAX_PER_SOURCE}\n     * @param globalCeiling     accepted handoffs per window across the JVM, 1..{@value #MAX_GLOBAL}\n     * @param maxTrackedSources bucket cap, 1..{@value #MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES}\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if a bound is out of range. Zero is refused rather than\n     *         silently read as \"unlimited\", and no argument is defaulted: an absent setting is the\n     *         caller's decision to make explicitly.\n     */\n    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources) {\n        this(windowSeconds, perSourceCeiling, globalCeiling, maxTrackedSources, defaultClock());\n    }\n\n    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources,\n                          Clock clock) {\n        if (windowSeconds < 1 || windowSeconds > MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"limiter window must be 1..\" + MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS + \" seconds\");\n        }\n        if (perSourceCeiling < 1 || perSourceCeiling > MAX_PER_SOURCE) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"per-source ceiling must be 1..\" + MAX_PER_SOURCE);\n        }\n        if (globalCeiling < 1 || globalCeiling > MAX_GLOBAL) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"global ceiling must be 1..\" + MAX_GLOBAL);\n        }\n        if (maxTrackedSources < 1 || maxTrackedSources > MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"tracked-source cap must be 1..\" + MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES);\n        }\n        if (clock == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"clock is required\");\n        }\n        long now = clock.millis();\n        this.windowMillis = windowSeconds * 1000L;\n        this.perSourceCeiling = perSourceCeiling;\n        // A request whose source address could not be established is the shape an attacker forges to\n        // obtain a fresh lane, so every such request shares one heavily reduced allowance.\n        this.unknownSourceCeiling = Math.max(1, perSourceCeiling / 4);\n        this.globalCeiling = globalCeiling;\n        this.maxTrackedSources = maxTrackedSources;\n        this.clock = clock;\n        this.global = new Window(now);\n    }\n\n    static Clock defaultClock() {\n        return new Clock() {\n            @Override\n            public long millis() {\n                // Monotonic: a wall-clock step (NTP, DST, VM resume) must neither extend a window\n                // enough to matter nor rewind one into a permanent block.\n                return System.nanoTime() / 1_000_000L;\n            }\n        };\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically tests and consumes allowance.\n     *\n     * @param source validated caller address; never used for anything but bucketing\n     * @return the decision; {@link Decision#ALLOWED} means allowance was consumed by this call\n     */\n    public Decision tryAcquire(ClientAddress source) {\n        if (source == null) {\n            // A caller that could not even name a source gets the shared unknown bucket, which is\n            // the most restricted lane. It is never an unlimited pass-through.\n            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        int ceiling = source.isUnknown() ? unknownSourceCeiling : perSourceCeiling;\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            long now = clock.millis();\n            Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n            if (window == null) {\n                if (windows.size() >= maxTrackedSources && !reclaimRolled(now)) {\n                    return Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED;\n                }\n                window = new Window(now);\n                windows.put(source.key(), window);\n                reclaimHand.addLast(source.key());\n            }\n            rollIfStale(window, now);\n            if (window.count >= ceiling) {\n                return Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE;\n            }\n            rollIfStale(global, now);\n            if (global.count >= globalCeiling) {\n                // Deliberately before incrementing: a saturated shared lane must not spend this\n                // source's own allowance, or a flood would lock out the clients it never touched.\n                return Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY;\n            }\n            window.count++;\n            global.count++;\n            return Decision.ALLOWED;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void rollIfStale(Window window, long now) {\n        if (now - window.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n            window.startMillis = now;\n            window.count = 0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reclaims one bucket whose window has rolled, looking at no more than\n     * {@link #RECLAIM_CANDIDATES} of them.\n     *\n     * <p>Called with the monitor held, so its cost is every other thread's latency. The obvious\n     * implementation -- walk the map and drop everything rolled -- is exactly the wrong shape here:\n     * the number of entries is the number of sources an attacker just created, so the attacker\n     * chooses how long the lock is held. Measured on this machine with a 50 000-bucket cap and every\n     * bucket still live, one admission cost 4.4 ms (17 ms worst) while four concurrent clients waited\n     * 1.5-7.6 ms for the same monitor, and because a partially rolled map stays at the cap the next\n     * admission pays it again. A flood was therefore worth ~1000x more to the server than an honest\n     * request, and the flood's own requests were the cheap-to-refuse ones.\n     *\n     * <p>The hand fixes that by making the work per admission a constant. Keys come out of the head\n     * in insertion order; a bucket that has rolled is dropped and the search stops, a bucket that has\n     * not goes to the tail for a second chance later. Live buckets at the head cannot starve rolled\n     * ones behind them, because every visit either frees memory or moves the hand on. Under a\n     * sustained flood the hand keeps sweeping across admissions instead of sweeping the whole map\n     * inside one.\n     *\n     * <p>Only rolled buckets are ever dropped, which is what makes this safe to do under load: an\n     * active source's counter is never taken away from it, so the cap cannot be turned into a way of\n     * wiping the state that limits an attacker.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} if a slot was freed\n     */\n    private boolean reclaimRolled(long now) {\n        for (int inspected = 0; inspected < RECLAIM_CANDIDATES; inspected++) {\n            String key = reclaimHand.pollFirst();\n            if (key == null) {\n                // Nothing queued to consider. The map is at the cap with no tracked bucket at all,\n                // which cannot happen while insertions are the only way in; treat it as \"no slot\".\n                return false;\n            }\n            Window candidate = windows.get(key);\n            if (candidate == null) {\n                // Stale queue entry: this bucket is already gone, so the queue just got shorter for\n                // free and there was no bucket to drop.\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (now - candidate.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n                windows.remove(key);\n                return true;\n            }\n            reclaimHand.addLast(key);\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Seconds until the caller's bucket rolls, for {@code Retry-After}.\n     *\n     * <p>A snapshot taken outside the monitor, and that is acceptable: it only tunes a hint in a\n     * response header, never an admission decision. Clamped to at least one second because\n     * {@code Retry-After: 0} invites an immediate retry loop.\n     */\n    public int retryAfterSeconds(ClientAddress source) {\n        if (source == null) {\n            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n        long now = clock.millis();\n        if (window == null) {\n            return (int) Math.max(1L, windowMillis / 1000L);\n        }\n        long elapsed = now - window.startMillis;\n        long remaining = windowMillis - elapsed;\n        if (remaining <= 0) {\n            return 1;\n        }\n        return (int) Math.max(1L, (remaining + 999L) / 1000L);\n    }\n\n    /** Number of tracked buckets; for tests and an operator metric, never a security decision. */\n    public int trackedSources() {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return windows.size();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Configuration summary. Contains no session material and no client addresses. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"AttemptLimiter[windowSeconds=\" + (windowMillis / 1000L) + \", perSource=\" + perSourceCeiling\n                + \", unknownSource=\" + unknownSourceCeiling + \", global=\" + globalCeiling\n                + \", maxTrackedSources=\" + maxTrackedSources + \"]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"f9e2d5d3-3d68-402b-bf00-8ffe09f5faaf","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_f36e8ab84e3944b8ae0eba8c","is_error":false,"content":"package com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport java.net.InetAddress;\nimport java.net.UnknownHostException;\nimport java.util.Locale;\n\n/**\n * Normalised client source address, used only as a rate-limit bucket key.\n *\n * <p>{@code request.getRemoteAddr()} is supplied by the container. It is unspoofable for a direct\n * TCP connection but fully attacker-chosen behind a proxy, so the container is treated as part of\n * the attack surface: the value is validated before use and never trusted to *identify* anyone.\n * Getting it wrong costs a slower retry for one bucket, never access, which is why it is safe to\n * key a limit on it at all (rules 1 and 3).\n *\n * <p>Canonicalisation matters for fairness. Without it {@code 0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1},\n * {@code 0::1} and {@code ::1} are three different buckets, and an attacker on one IPv6 address\n * could spend the allowance of another by varying textual form. Canonical text is also what keeps\n * the bucket map from growing without bound on cosmetic variants.\n *\n * <p>{@code X-Forwarded-For} and every other {@code * -For} header are deliberately never read:\n * see the README for the deployment condition under which that must be revisited.\n */\npublic final class ClientAddress {\n\n    /**\n     * Address used when the container supplies nothing usable. One shared bucket is the\n     * least-privilege choice: a missing source must not become an unlimited lane, and it must not\n     * create an unbounded number of lanes either.\n     */\n    public static final ClientAddress UNKNOWN = new ClientAddress(\"unknown\", 0, true);\n\n    private static final int MAX_RAW_LENGTH = 64;\n    private static final int MAX_IPV6_LENGTH = 45;\n\n    /**\n     * True for {@code d.d.d.d} with four groups of one to three digits, each 0-255. Refuses the\n     * shorthand forms {@code InetAddress} tolerates, so one host cannot open several buckets for one\n     * address by varying how it is written.\n     */\n    private static boolean isDottedQuad(String text) {\n        int groups = 0;\n        int digits = 0;\n        int value = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i < text.length(); i++) {\n            char c = text.charAt(i);\n            if (c == '.') {\n                if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n                    return false;\n                }\n                groups++;\n                digits = 0;\n                value = 0;\n            } else if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {\n                // A leading zero is tolerated in text but the numeric bound below is what matters.\n                digits++;\n                value = value * 10 + (c - '0');\n            } else {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        groups++;\n        return groups == 4;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Full grammar of an IPv6 <i>literal</i>: groups of one to four hex digits, at most one\n     * {@code ::}, at most eight 16-bit groups in total, an optional trailing dotted quad, and nothing\n     * else. {@code -1} means malformed.\n     *\n     * <p>This exists because \"contains a colon\" is not a shape check. The character allowlist permits\n     * hex digits and colons, so text like {@code a:b}, {@code 1234:5678} or {@code deadbeef:cafe}\n     * looks like an address to anything that only counts dots and colons, and\n     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} then hands it to the operating-system resolver as a hostname.\n     * Measured here: those forms cost 14-41 us each in a lookup attempt, against 3 us for a real\n     * literal, and they cost it on every request before any throttle has been consulted -- an\n     * attacker-chosen amount of resolver work per packet. Rejecting them here also means no name\n     * beyond this method's grammar can ever reach a resolver from a request.\n     *\n     * <p>Interface scope ids ({@code fe80::1%9}) never reach here: {@code %} is outside the character\n     * allowlist, so they are refused as unparseable and land in the shared unknown bucket.\n     *\n     * @param run        one side of the {@code ::}, or the whole address when there is none\n     * @param atEnd      whether this run ends the address, which is where a dotted quad may appear\n     * @return the number of 16-bit groups the run accounts for, or {@code -1}\n     */\n    private static int ipv6Groups(String run, boolean atEnd) {\n        if (run.isEmpty()) {\n            return 0;\n        }\n        String[] parts = run.split(\":\", -1);\n        int total = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {\n            String group = parts[i];\n            if (group.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n                // The mixed form is legal only as the last two groups of the whole address.\n                if (!atEnd || i != parts.length - 1 || !isDottedQuad(group)) {\n                    return -1;\n                }\n                total += 2;\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (group.isEmpty() || group.length() > 4) {\n                return -1;\n            }\n            for (int c = 0; c < group.length(); c++) {\n                char digit = group.charAt(c);\n                boolean hex = (digit >= '0' && digit <= '9')\n                        || (digit >= 'a' && digit <= 'f')\n                        || (digit >= 'A' && digit <= 'F');\n                if (!hex) {\n                    return -1;\n                }\n            }\n            total++;\n        }\n        return total;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether {@code text} is a complete IPv6 literal, per {@link #ipv6Groups}.\n     *\n     * <p>Stricter than the JDK where the two disagree: a container emits the expanded\n     * eight-group form, so accepting {@code ::} compression is convenience rather than necessity, and\n     * anything the JDK would tolerate beyond this grammar (scope ids, embedded text) is refused here\n     * and degrades to the shared bucket instead.\n     */\n    private static boolean isIpv6Literal(String text) {\n        int compress = text.indexOf(\"::\");\n        if (compress < 0) {\n            if (text.indexOf(':') < 0) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            return ipv6Groups(text, true) == 8;\n        }\n        if (text.indexOf(\"::\", compress + 1) >= 0) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        int head = ipv6Groups(text.substring(0, compress), false);\n        int tail = ipv6Groups(text.substring(compress + 2), true);\n        // '::' has to stand for at least one all-zero group, so at most six may be written out.\n        if (head < 0 || tail < 0 || head + tail > 6) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (text.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n            // Inside a compressed address exactly one mixed spelling is accepted: the one a\n            // dual-stack container emits for an IPv4 peer. \"::127.0.0.1\" and its like (the IPv4\n            // *compatible* form, obsoleted by RFC 4291) are refused outright rather than\n            // canonicalised on a guess -- they mean different things to different stacks, and\n            // folding them is how \"::1\" used to arrive as the bucket key \"0.0.0.1/32\".\n            return isIpv4MappedText(text);\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /** The one compressed mixed form that is admitted: {@code ::ffff:d.d.d.d}, either hex case. */\n    private static boolean isIpv4MappedText(String text) {\n        String prefix = \"::ffff:\";\n        return text.regionMatches(true, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length())\n                && isDottedQuad(text.substring(prefix.length()));\n    }\n\n    private final String canonical;\n    private final int prefixBits;\n    private final boolean unknown;\n\n    private ClientAddress(String canonical, int prefixBits, boolean unknown) {\n        this.canonical = canonical;\n        this.prefixBits = prefixBits;\n        this.unknown = unknown;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Parses and canonicalises a textual address.\n     *\n     * <p>The shape is checked before the JDK parser is consulted, because\n     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} also accepts {@code 517996801}, {@code 0x7f.1}, {@code 10.1} and\n     * similar shorthand forms. A container never emits those, and admitting them would mean one host\n     * could hold several buckets for the same address purely by textual form -- the exact unfairness\n     * this class exists to prevent.\n     *\n     * @return the canonical form, or {@link #UNKNOWN} for absent, oversized, non-textual or\n     *         unparseable input. No exception is thrown: a malformed address must degrade to a\n     *         shared limited bucket, never to an unlimited one, and never to a stack trace.\n     */\n    public static ClientAddress parse(String remoteAddr) {\n        if (remoteAddr == null || remoteAddr.isEmpty() || remoteAddr.length() > MAX_RAW_LENGTH) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        // Reject structural surprises before the parser sees them, so no bracket, scope-id,\n        // percent-encoding or control character can reach InetAddress parsing. This loop is redundant\n        // with the two grammars below -- they admit the same five classes of character and nothing else\n        // -- and it is kept as the cheap gate that says so in one line. The harness sweeps every\n        // character this refuses against both grammars, so if either grammar is ever loosened the\n        // redundancy ends in a failing test rather than in a resolver round trip per request.\n        for (int i = 0; i < remoteAddr.length(); i++) {\n            char c = remoteAddr.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F')\n                    || c == '.' || c == ':';\n            if (!allowed) {\n                return UNKNOWN;\n            }\n        }\n        boolean ipv6 = remoteAddr.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n        if (ipv6) {\n            if (remoteAddr.length() > MAX_IPV6_LENGTH || !isIpv6Literal(remoteAddr)) {\n                return UNKNOWN;\n            }\n        } else if (!isDottedQuad(remoteAddr)) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        InetAddress address;\n        try {\n            // Only two shapes can arrive here -- a four-group dotted quad and a complete IPv6\n            // literal -- and both are literals, so getByName takes its parse-without-a-lookup path\n            // and the resolver is never consulted. That guarantee is what the two shape checks exist\n            // to provide: a DNS round trip per request would turn the limiter into an amplifier\n            // driven by an attacker-supplied address, and reverse lookups are never requested either.\n            address = InetAddress.getByName(remoteAddr);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException malformed) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        } catch (UnknownHostException notAnAddress) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet4Address) {\n            return new ClientAddress(address.getHostAddress(), 32, false);\n        }\n        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet6Address) {\n            // A dual-stack container reports an IPv4 peer as ::ffff:a.b.c.d. That is the same host as\n            // a.b.c.d and must not get a second lane, so the mapped form folds onto the IPv4 bucket.\n            // The deprecated IPv4-*compatible* form (::a.b.c.d, obsoleted by RFC 4291) is deliberately\n            // not folded any more: folding it is what used to turn \"::1\" into the bucket key\n            // \"0.0.0.1/32\" -- an IPv6 loopback peer asserting an IPv4 identity, merged with an address\n            // that never appears on a wire. Nothing a container actually emits needs that fold, so an\n            // unroutable legacy spelling now falls into the plain /64 lane like any other v6 peer.\n            if (isIpv4Mapped(bytes)) {\n                return new ClientAddress(\n                        (bytes[12] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[13] & 0xFF) + \".\"\n                                + (bytes[14] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[15] & 0xFF),\n                        32, false);\n            }\n            // /64 is the allocation unit an attacker commonly controls wholesale (and the unit a\n            // /64-routing site hands out), so limiting per address would be a joke while limiting\n            // per /64 stays fair to a normal dual-stack client. One consequence is worth writing down\n            // instead of leaving to chance: every address whose top 64 bits are zero -- \"::1\" first --\n            // shares the single lane \"0:0:0:0/64\". Those addresses are not reachable over a public\n            // link, so that lane holds local processes and port-forwarded sockets, and merging them\n            // is the conservative direction: it can delay a local caller, it cannot hand anyone a\n            // wider lane than their own address would have had.\n            return new ClientAddress(canonicalIpv6Prefix(bytes), 64, false);\n        }\n        return UNKNOWN;\n    }\n\n    /** True for {@code ::ffff:a.b.c.d}, the form a dual-stack socket reports an IPv4 peer as. */\n    private static boolean isIpv4Mapped(byte[] bytes) {\n        if (bytes.length != 16) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n            if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return bytes[10] == (byte) 0xFF && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xFF;\n    }\n\n    /** Lower-case canonical text of the top 64 bits, e.g. {@code 2001:db8:0:1}. */\n    private static String canonicalIpv6Prefix(byte[] bytes) {\n        StringBuilder text = new StringBuilder(19);\n        for (int group = 0; group < 4; group++) {\n            if (group > 0) {\n                text.append(':');\n            }\n            int value = ((bytes[group * 2] & 0xFF) << 8) | (bytes[group * 2 + 1] & 0xFF);\n            text.append(Integer.toHexString(value));\n        }\n        return text.toString().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n    }\n\n    /** Bucket key: canonical text plus the prefix length, so v4 and v6 lanes never collide. */\n    public String key() {\n        return canonical + '/' + prefixBits;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether this bucket shares the allowance with every other unknown-source request. Callers\n     * apply a stricter ceiling to it: a request whose source cannot be established is exactly the\n     * shape an attacker would forge.\n     */\n    public boolean isUnknown() {\n        return unknown;\n    }\n\n    /** Canonical text only. It is the caller's own address, so it is not a secret. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return canonical;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(Object other) {\n other).key());\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int hashCode() {\n        return key().hashCode();\n
