Rule 3 requires throttling, and requires it to be per source as well as\n * global.\n *\n * <p><b>Why a per-source limit cannot lock a legitimate user out.</b> Three properties, all tested:\n * <ol>\n *   <li><i>The ceiling is a per-window allowance, not a consumption budget.</i> A denied attempt does\n *       not consume allowance, so an attacker flooding requests cannot spend the victim's quota; only\n *       admitted requests advance the counter. Note what this does <i>not</i> say: an attempt the\n *       session store refuses still consumed allowance, because it was a real authentication attempt.\n *       Making those free would hand the attacker free probing, which is the opposite of the limit.</li>\n *   <li><i>The window rolls.</i> A bucket that filled recovers automatically once it rolls over, so a\n *       limit can never be a permanent denial of service.</li>\n *   <li><i>Global and per-source are separate.</i> Exhausting the global lane leaves the source's own\n *       allowance untouched, so a flood cannot leave a victim <i>permanently</i> blocked behind a\n *       personal wall they never built.</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <p><b>What the global ceiling is for, stated honestly.</b> It is a circuit breaker on round trips\n * to the session store, and like every circuit breaker it degrades everyone inside its scope while\n * it is open: a flood spread over enough distinct sources can saturate it and make honest clients\n * retry (rule 3 asks for a global limit precisely because the alternative is unbounded store load).\n * The two properties that keep that cost bounded are that it never spends anyone's personal\n * allowance (property 3 above) and that it clears by itself when the window rolls (property 2). It\n * must therefore be configured above normal peak traffic; {@code SessionHandoffSettings} enforces\n * {@code global >= perSource} and the default gives 2 000 admitted handoffs per 60 s window against\n * 30 per source, so saturating it takes an attacker ~67 concurrent sources.\n *\n * <p><b>Atomicity (rule 7).</b> Read, roll-over, compare and increment happen inside one monitor.\n * A check-then-act split here is exactly how a fixed-window limiter becomes useless: N concurrent\n * requests would all read {@code count = limit - 1} and all be admitted. For the monitor to be a\n * tool rather than a bottleneck, <i>every</i> path through it must cost O(1), including the one that\n * reclaims buckets at the tracking cap. That is why reclamation is a bounded second-chance hand\n * (see {@link #reclaimRolled}) rather than a sweep: a sweep costs time proportional to how many\n * sources the attacker just created, which is the one number an attacker controls, and every honest\n * request in the JVM then queues behind it.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded state.</b> Buckets are keyed by normalised source and the map is capped; when a\n * flood introduces more distinct sources than the cap, the limiter sheds requests instead of growing\n * memory without bound. Failing closed is correct here because a memory-exhausted container cannot\n * serve anyone.\n */\npublic final class AttemptLimiter {\n\n    /** Upper bound on the configurable window, so a typo cannot create a permanent block. */\n    public static final int MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS = 3600;\n    /** Upper bound on the per-source ceiling, so a typo cannot make the limit meaningless. */\n    public static final int MAX_PER_SOURCE = 1000;\n    /** Upper bound on the global ceiling, for the same reason. */\n    public static final int MAX_GLOBAL = 100000;\n    /** Upper bound on tracked buckets, so a source-spoofing flood cannot exhaust the heap. */\n    public static final int MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES = 200000;\n\n    /** Outcome of one admission test. */\n    public enum Decision {\n        /** The request may proceed and consume allowance. */\n        ALLOWED,\n        /** This source has used its allowance for the current window. */\n        LIMITED_PER_SOURCE,\n        /** The shared lane is saturated; this source's own allowance is untouched. */\n        LIMITED_GLOBALLY,\n        /** Too many distinct sources are being tracked; shed rather than grow unbounded. */\n        LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED\n    }\n\n    /** Time source, injected so the window behaviour can be tested without sleeping. */\n    public interface Clock {\n        long millis();\n    }\n\n    private static final class Window {\n        private long startMillis;\n        private int count;\n\n        private Window(long startMillis) {\n            this.startMillis = startMillis;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window> windows =\n            new java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window>();\n\n    /**\n     * Keys in insertion order, consumed from the head by {@link #reclaimRolled}. Guarded by\n     * {@link #monitor} like every other piece of limiter state, and it holds at most one key per\n     * tracked bucket, so it cannot grow past {@link #maxTrackedSources} either.\n     */\n    private final java.util.ArrayDeque<String> reclaimHand = new java.util.ArrayDeque<String>();\n\n    /**\n     * Buckets the reclaim hand may look at for one admission. This is the constant that keeps the\n     * monitor's hold time independent of how many sources are being tracked: measured here, one\n     * admission at the cap costs single-digit microseconds whatever the cap is, where a full sweep\n     * cost milliseconds per admission and made every other thread in the JVM wait for it.\n     */\n    private static final int RECLAIM_CANDIDATES = 64;\n\n    /** The shared lane. Counted under the same monitor as every per-source bucket. */\n    private final Window global;\n\n    private final Object monitor = new Object();\n\n    private final long windowMillis;\n    private final int perSourceCeiling;\n    private final int unknownSourceCeiling;\n    private final int globalCeiling;\n    private final int maxTrackedSources;\n    private final Clock clock;\n\n    /**\n     * @param windowSeconds     rolling window length, 1..{@value #MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS}\n     * @param perSourceCeiling  accepted handoffs per window per source, 1..{@value #MAX_PER_SOURCE}\n     * @param globalCeiling     accepted handoffs per window across the JVM, 1..{@value #MAX_GLOBAL}\n     * @param maxTrackedSources bucket cap, 1..{@value #MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES}\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if a bound is out of range. Zero is refused rather than\n     *         silently read as \"unlimited\", and no argument is defaulted: an absent setting is the\n     *         caller's decision to make explicitly.\n     */\n    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources) {\n        this(windowSeconds, perSourceCeiling, globalCeiling, maxTrackedSources, defaultClock());\n    }\n\n    public AttemptLimiter(int windowSeconds, int perSourceCeiling, int globalCeiling, int maxTrackedSources,\n                          Clock clock) {\n        if (windowSeconds < 1 || windowSeconds > MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"limiter window must be 1..\" + MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS + \" seconds\");\n        }\n        if (perSourceCeiling < 1 || perSourceCeiling > MAX_PER_SOURCE) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"per-source ceiling must be 1..\" + MAX_PER_SOURCE);\n        }\n        if (globalCeiling < 1 || globalCeiling > MAX_GLOBAL) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"global ceiling must be 1..\" + MAX_GLOBAL);\n        }\n        if (maxTrackedSources < 1 || maxTrackedSources > MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"tracked-source cap must be 1..\" + MAX_TRACKED_SOURCES);\n        }\n        if (clock == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"clock is required\");\n        }\n        long now = clock.millis();\n        this.windowMillis = windowSeconds * 1000L;\n        this.perSourceCeiling = perSourceCeiling;\n        // A request whose source address could not be established is the shape an attacker forges to\n        // obtain a fresh lane, so every such request shares one heavily reduced allowance.\n        this.unknownSourceCeiling = Math.max(1, perSourceCeiling / 4);\n        this.globalCeiling = globalCeiling;\n        this.maxTrackedSources = maxTrackedSources;\n        this.clock = clock;\n        this.global = new Window(now);\n    }\n\n    static Clock defaultClock() {\n        return new Clock() {\n            @Override\n            public long millis() {\n                // Monotonic: a wall-clock step (NTP, DST, VM resume) must neither extend a window\n                // enough to matter nor rewind one into a permanent block.\n                return System.nanoTime() / 1_000_000L;\n            }\n        };\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically tests and consumes allowance.\n     *\n     * @param source validated caller address; never used for anything but bucketing\n     * @return the decision; {@link Decision#ALLOWED} means allowance was consumed by this call\n     */\n    public Decision tryAcquire(ClientAddress source) {\n        if (source == null) {\n            // A caller that could not even name a source gets the shared unknown bucket, which is\n            // the most restricted lane. It is never an unlimited pass-through.\n            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        int ceiling = source.isUnknown() ? unknownSourceCeiling : perSourceCeiling;\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            long now = clock.millis();\n            Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n            if (window == null) {\n                if (windows.size() >= maxTrackedSources && !reclaimRolled(now)) {\n                    return Decision.LIMITED_TRACKING_SATURATED;\n                }\n                window = new Window(now);\n                windows.put(source.key(), window);\n                reclaimHand.addLast(source.key());\n            }\n            rollIfStale(window, now);\n            if (window.count >= ceiling) {\n                return Decision.LIMITED_PER_SOURCE;\n            }\n            rollIfStale(global, now);\n            if (global.count >= globalCeiling) {\n                // Deliberately before incrementing: a saturated shared lane must not spend this\n                // source's own allowance, or a flood would lock out the clients it never touched.\n                return Decision.LIMITED_GLOBALLY;\n            }\n            window.count++;\n            global.count++;\n            return Decision.ALLOWED;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void rollIfStale(Window window, long now) {\n        if (now - window.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n            window.startMillis = now;\n            window.count = 0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reclaims one bucket whose window has rolled, looking at no more than\n     * {@link #RECLAIM_CANDIDATES} of them.\n     *\n     * <p>Called with the monitor held, so its cost is every other thread's latency. The obvious\n     * implementation -- walk the map and drop everything rolled -- is exactly the wrong shape here:\n     * the number of entries is the number of sources an attacker just created, so the attacker\n     * chooses how long the lock is held. Measured on this machine with a 50 000-bucket cap and every\n     * bucket still live, one admission cost 4.4 ms (17 ms worst) while four concurrent clients waited\n     * 1.5-7.6 ms for the same monitor, and because a partially rolled map stays at the cap the next\n     * admission pays it again. A flood was therefore worth ~1000x more to the server than an honest\n     * request, and the flood's own requests were the cheap-to-refuse ones.\n     *\n     * <p>The hand fixes that by making the work per admission a constant. Keys come out of the head\n     * in insertion order; a bucket that has rolled is dropped and the search stops, a bucket that has\n     * not goes to the tail for a second chance later. Live buckets at the head cannot starve rolled\n     * ones behind them, because every visit either frees memory or moves the hand on. Under a\n     * sustained flood the hand keeps sweeping across admissions instead of sweeping the whole map\n     * inside one.\n     *\n     * <p>Only rolled buckets are ever dropped, which is what makes this safe to do under load: an\n     * active source's counter is never taken away from it, so the cap cannot be turned into a way of\n     * wiping the state that limits an attacker.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} if a slot was freed\n     */\n    private boolean reclaimRolled(long now) {\n        for (int inspected = 0; inspected < RECLAIM_CANDIDATES; inspected++) {\n            String key = reclaimHand.pollFirst();\n            if (key == null) {\n                // Nothing queued to consider. The map is at the cap with no tracked bucket at all,\n                // which cannot happen while insertions are the only way in; treat it as \"no slot\".\n                return false;\n            }\n            Window candidate = windows.get(key);\n            if (candidate == null) {\n                // Stale queue entry: this bucket is already gone, so the queue just got shorter for\n                // free and there was no bucket to drop.\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (now - candidate.startMillis >= windowMillis) {\n                windows.remove(key);\n                return true;\n            }\n            reclaimHand.addLast(key);\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Seconds until the caller's bucket rolls, for {@code Retry-After}.\n     *\n     * <p>A snapshot taken outside the monitor, and that is acceptable: it only tunes a hint in a\n     * response header, never an admission decision. Clamped to at least one second because\n     * {@code Retry-After: 0} invites an immediate retry loop.\n     */\n    public int retryAfterSeconds(ClientAddress source) {\n        if (source == null) {\n            source = ClientAddress.UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        Window window = windows.get(source.key());\n        long now = clock.millis();\n        if (window == null) {\n            return (int) Math.max(1L, windowMillis / 1000L);\n        }\n        long elapsed = now - window.startMillis;\n        long remaining = windowMillis - elapsed;\n        if (remaining <= 0) {\n            return 1;\n        }\n        return (int) Math.max(1L, (remaining + 999L) / 1000L);\n    }\n\n    /** Number of tracked buckets; for tests and an operator metric, never a security decision. */\n    public int trackedSources() {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return windows.size();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Configuration summary. Contains no session material and no client addresses. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"AttemptLimiter[windowSeconds=\" + (windowMillis / 1000L) + \", perSource=\" + perSourceCeiling\n                + \", unknownSource=\" + unknownSourceCeiling + \", global=\" + globalCeiling\n                + \", maxTrackedSources=\" + maxTrackedSources + \"]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"fd2c6f7e-ef5a-4a77-8014-c8dba1183b52","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"fd2c6f7e-ef5a-4a77-8014-c8dba1183b52","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"This is the last core source file — ClientAddress.java. It is unspoofable for a direct\n * TCP connection but fully attacker-chosen behind a proxy, so the container is treated as part of\n * the attack surface: the value is validated before use and never trusted to *identify* anyone.\n * Getting it wrong costs a slower retry for one bucket, never access, which is why it is safe to\n * key a limit on it at all (rules 1 and 3).\n *\n * <p>Canonicalisation matters for fairness. Without it {@code 0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1},\n * {@code 0::1} and {@code ::1} are three different buckets, and an attacker on one IPv6 address\n * could spend the allowance of another by varying textual form. Canonical text is also what keeps\n * the bucket map from growing without bound on cosmetic variants.\n *\n * <p>{@code X-Forwarded-For} and every other {@code * -For} header are deliberately never read:\n * see the README for the deployment condition under which that must be revisited.\n */\npublic final class ClientAddress {\n\n    /**\n     * Address used when the container supplies nothing usable. One shared bucket is the\n     * least-privilege choice: a missing source must not become an unlimited lane, and it must not\n     * create an unbounded number of lanes either.\n     */\n    public static final ClientAddress UNKNOWN = new ClientAddress(\"unknown\", 0, true);\n\n    private static final int MAX_RAW_LENGTH = 64;\n    private static final int MAX_IPV6_LENGTH = 45;\n\n    /**\n     * True for {@code d.d.d.d} with four groups of one to three digits, each 0-255. Refuses the\n     * shorthand forms {@code InetAddress} tolerates, so one host cannot open several buckets for one\n     * address by varying how it is written.\n     */\n    private static boolean isDottedQuad(String text) {\n        int groups = 0;\n        int digits = 0;\n        int value = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i < text.length(); i++) {\n            char c = text.charAt(i);\n            if (c == '.') {\n                if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n                    return false;\n                }\n                groups++;\n                digits = 0;\n                value = 0;\n            } else if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {\n                // A leading zero is tolerated in text but the numeric bound below is what matters.\n                digits++;\n                value = value * 10 + (c - '0');\n            } else {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        if (digits == 0 || digits > 3 || value > 255) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        groups++;\n        return groups == 4;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Full grammar of an IPv6 <i>literal</i>: groups of one to four hex digits, at most one\n     * {@code ::}, at most eight 16-bit groups in total, an optional trailing dotted quad, and nothing\n     * else. {@code -1} means malformed.\n     *\n     * <p>This exists because \"contains a colon\" is not a shape check. The character allowlist permits\n     * hex digits and colons, so text like {@code a:b}, {@code 1234:5678} or {@code deadbeef:cafe}\n     * looks like an address to anything that only counts dots and colons, and\n     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} then hands it to the operating-system resolver as a hostname.\n     * Measured here: those forms cost 14-41 us each in a lookup attempt, against 3 us for a real\n     * literal, and they cost it on every request before any throttle has been consulted -- an\n     * attacker-chosen amount of resolver work per packet. Rejecting them here also means no name\n     * beyond this method's grammar can ever reach a resolver from a request.\n     *\n     * <p>Interface scope ids ({@code fe80::1%9}) never reach here: {@code %} is outside the character\n     * allowlist, so they are refused as unparseable and land in the shared unknown bucket.\n     *\n     * @param run        one side of the {@code ::}, or the whole address when there is none\n     * @param atEnd      whether this run ends the address, which is where a dotted quad may appear\n     * @return the number of 16-bit groups the run accounts for, or {@code -1}\n     */\n    private static int ipv6Groups(String run, boolean atEnd) {\n        if (run.isEmpty()) {\n            return 0;\n        }\n        String[] parts = run.split(\":\", -1);\n        int total = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {\n            String group = parts[i];\n            if (group.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n                // The mixed form is legal only as the last two groups of the whole address.\n                if (!atEnd || i != parts.length - 1 || !isDottedQuad(group)) {\n                    return -1;\n                }\n                total += 2;\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (group.isEmpty() || group.length() > 4) {\n                return -1;\n            }\n            for (int c = 0; c < group.length(); c++) {\n                char digit = group.charAt(c);\n                boolean hex = (digit >= '0' && digit <= '9')\n                        || (digit >= 'a' && digit <= 'f')\n                        || (digit >= 'A' && digit <= 'F');\n                if (!hex) {\n                    return -1;\n                }\n            }\n            total++;\n        }\n        return total;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether {@code text} is a complete IPv6 literal, per {@link #ipv6Groups}.\n     *\n     * <p>Stricter than the JDK where the two disagree: a container emits the expanded\n     * eight-group form, so accepting {@code ::} compression is convenience rather than necessity, and\n     * anything the JDK would tolerate beyond this grammar (scope ids, embedded text) is refused here\n     * and degrades to the shared bucket instead.\n     */\n    private static boolean isIpv6Literal(String text) {\n        int compress = text.indexOf(\"::\");\n        if (compress < 0) {\n            if (text.indexOf(':') < 0) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            return ipv6Groups(text, true) == 8;\n        }\n        if (text.indexOf(\"::\", compress + 1) >= 0) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        int head = ipv6Groups(text.substring(0, compress), false);\n        int tail = ipv6Groups(text.substring(compress + 2), true);\n        // '::' has to stand for at least one all-zero group, so at most six may be written out.\n        if (head < 0 || tail < 0 || head + tail > 6) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (text.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n            // Inside a compressed address exactly one mixed spelling is accepted: the one a\n            // dual-stack container emits for an IPv4 peer. \"::127.0.0.1\" and its like (the IPv4\n            // *compatible* form, obsoleted by RFC 4291) are refused outright rather than\n            // canonicalised on a guess -- they mean different things to different stacks, and\n            // folding them is how \"::1\" used to arrive as the bucket key \"0.0.0.1/32\".\n            return isIpv4MappedText(text);\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /** The one compressed mixed form that is admitted: {@code ::ffff:d.d.d.d}, either hex case. */\n    private static boolean isIpv4MappedText(String text) {\n        String prefix = \"::ffff:\";\n        return text.regionMatches(true, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length())\n                && isDottedQuad(text.substring(prefix.length()));\n    }\n\n    private final String canonical;\n    private final int prefixBits;\n    private final boolean unknown;\n\n    private ClientAddress(String canonical, int prefixBits, boolean unknown) {\n        this.canonical = canonical;\n        this.prefixBits = prefixBits;\n        this.unknown = unknown;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Parses and canonicalises a textual address.\n     *\n     * <p>The shape is checked before the JDK parser is consulted, because\n     * {@code InetAddress.getByName} also accepts {@code 517996801}, {@code 0x7f.1}, {@code 10.1} and\n     * similar shorthand forms. A container never emits those, and admitting them would mean one host\n     * could hold several buckets for the same address purely by textual form -- the exact unfairness\n     * this class exists to prevent.\n     *\n     * @return the canonical form, or {@link #UNKNOWN} for absent, oversized, non-textual or\n     *         unparseable input. No exception is thrown: a malformed address must degrade to a\n     *         shared limited bucket, never to an unlimited one, and never to a stack trace.\n     */\n    public static ClientAddress parse(String remoteAddr) {\n        if (remoteAddr == null || remoteAddr.isEmpty() || remoteAddr.length() > MAX_RAW_LENGTH) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        // Reject structural surprises before the parser sees them, so no bracket, scope-id,\n        // percent-encoding or control character can reach InetAddress parsing. This loop is redundant\n        // with the two grammars below -- they admit the same five classes of character and nothing else\n        // -- and it is kept as the cheap gate that says so in one line. The harness sweeps every\n        // character this refuses against both grammars, so if either grammar is ever loosened the\n        // redundancy ends in a failing test rather than in a resolver round trip per request.\n        for (int i = 0; i < remoteAddr.length(); i++) {\n            char c = remoteAddr.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F')\n                    || c == '.' || c == ':';\n            if (!allowed) {\n                return UNKNOWN;\n            }\n        }\n        boolean ipv6 = remoteAddr.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n        if (ipv6) {\n            if (remoteAddr.length() > MAX_IPV6_LENGTH || !isIpv6Literal(remoteAddr)) {\n                return UNKNOWN;\n            }\n        } else if (!isDottedQuad(remoteAddr)) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        InetAddress address;\n        try {\n            // Only two shapes can arrive here -- a four-group dotted quad and a complete IPv6\n            // literal -- and both are literals, so getByName takes its parse-without-a-lookup path\n            // and the resolver is never consulted. That guarantee is what the two shape checks exist\n            // to provide: a DNS round trip per request would turn the limiter into an amplifier\n            // driven by an attacker-supplied address, and reverse lookups are never requested either.\n            address = InetAddress.getByName(remoteAddr);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException malformed) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        } catch (UnknownHostException notAnAddress) {\n            return UNKNOWN;\n        }\n        byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet4Address) {\n            return new ClientAddress(address.getHostAddress(), 32, false);\n        }\n        if (address instanceof java.net.Inet6Address) {\n            // A dual-stack container reports an IPv4 peer as ::ffff:a.b.c.d. That is the same host as\n            // a.b.c.d and must not get a second lane, so the mapped form folds onto the IPv4 bucket.\n            // The deprecated IPv4-*compatible* form (::a.b.c.d, obsoleted by RFC 4291) is deliberately\n            // not folded any more: folding it is what used to turn \"::1\" into the bucket key\n            // \"0.0.0.1/32\" -- an IPv6 loopback peer asserting an IPv4 identity, merged with an address\n            // that never appears on a wire. Nothing a container actually emits needs that fold, so an\n            // unroutable legacy spelling now falls into the plain /64 lane like any other v6 peer.\n            if (isIpv4Mapped(bytes)) {\n                return new ClientAddress(\n                        (bytes[12] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[13] & 0xFF) + \".\"\n                                + (bytes[14] & 0xFF) + \".\" + (bytes[15] & 0xFF),\n                        32, false);\n            }\n            // /64 is the allocation unit an attacker commonly controls wholesale (and the unit a\n            // /64-routing site hands out), so limiting per address would be a joke while limiting\n            // per /64 stays fair to a normal dual-stack client. One consequence is worth writing down\n            // instead of leaving to chance: every address whose top 64 bits are zero -- \"::1\" first --\n            // shares the single lane \"0:0:0:0/64\". Those addresses are not reachable over a public\n            // link, so that lane holds local processes and port-forwarded sockets, and merging them\n            // is the conservative direction: it can delay a local caller, it cannot hand anyone a\n            // wider lane than their own address would have had.\n            return new ClientAddress(canonicalIpv6Prefix(bytes), 64, false);\n        }\n        return UNKNOWN;\n    }\n\n    /** True for {@code ::ffff:a.b.c.d}, the form a dual-stack socket reports an IPv4 peer as. */\n    private static boolean isIpv4Mapped(byte[] bytes) {\n        if (bytes.length != 16) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n            if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return bytes[10] == (byte) 0xFF && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xFF;\n    }\n\n    /** Lower-case canonical text of the top 64 bits, e.g. {@code 2001:db8:0:1}. */\n    private static String canonicalIpv6Prefix(byte[] bytes) {\n        StringBuilder text = new StringBuilder(19);\n        for (int group = 0; group < 4; group++) {\n            if (group > 0) {\n                text.append(':');\n            }\n            int value = ((bytes[group * 2] & 0xFF) << 8) | (bytes[group * 2 + 1] & 0xFF);\n            text.append(Integer.toHexString(value));\n        }\n        return text.toString().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n    }\n\n    /** Bucket key: canonical text plus the prefix length, so v4 and v6 lanes never collide. */\n    public String key() {\n        return canonical + '/' + prefixBits;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether this bucket shares the allowance with every other unknown-source request. Callers\n     * apply a stricter ceiling to it: a request whose source cannot be established is exactly the\n     * shape an attacker would forge.\n     */\n    public boolean isUnknown() {\n        return unknown;\n    }\n\n    /** Canonical text only. It is the caller's own address, so it is not a secret. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return canonical;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public boolean equals(Object other) {\n        if (this == other) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        if (!(other instanceof ClientAddress)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        return key().equals(((ClientAddress) other).key());\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int hashCode() {\n        return key().hashCode();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"f6440c47-cdb3-4d9f-867d-fd0952e64d66","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"f6440c47-cdb3-4d9f-867d-fd0952e64d66","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I've now read all 16 production classes in `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/`. com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.HashSet;\nimport java.util.Locale;\nimport java.util.Set;\n\n/**\n * Validated, immutable description of the session cookie this component emits.\n *\n * <p>Only three things are operator-selectable: the cookie name, the cookie path and the SameSite\n * mode. {@code Secure} and {@code HttpOnly} are invariants of this class, not configuration, so a\n * mis-deployed property cannot produce a script-readable or transport-unencrypted session cookie.\n * The cookie is always host-only (no {@code Domain}) and always a browser-session cookie (no\n * {@code Max-Age}/{@code Expires}), which bounds session lifetime by the trusted component's own\n * server-side expiry rather than by anything a client could extend or a misconfiguration could\n * stretch.\n *\n * <p>Construction is all-or-nothing. A malformed or unrecognised setting raises\n * {@link IllegalArgumentException} and the component refuses to start: there is no permissive\n * default for a security-relevant attribute and no silent coercion (a typo such as\n * {@code SameSide=Strict} fails loudly at deployment instead of quietly weakening the cookie).\n * Only a genuinely absent or empty value falls back to the hardened default below.\n */\npublic final class SessionCookiePolicy {\n\n    /**\n     * Default name uses the {@code __Host-} cookie prefix, which tells a conforming browser to\n     * discard the cookie unless it is Secure, host-only and {@code Path=/}. That is a second,\n     * client-enforced layer over the invariants this class already guarantees.\n     */\n    public static final String DEFAULT_NAME = \"__Host-PORTAL_SESSION\";\n\n    /** Default path: the application root, so the cookie accompanies every app URL and nothing else. */\n    public static final String DEFAULT_PATH = \"/\";\n\n    public static final String DEFAULT_SAME_SITE = \"Strict\";\n\n    /** Names this component refuses, so it can never emit a cookie named after a protocol keyword. */\n    private static final Set<String> RESERVED_NAMES = new HashSet<>(Arrays.asList(\n            \"set-cookie\", \"cookie\", \"expires\", \"max-age\", \"domain\", \"path\", \"secure\", \"httponly\",\n            \"samesite\", \"version\", \"comment\"));\n\n    private static final int MAX_NAME_LENGTH = 64;\n    private static final int MAX_PATH_LENGTH = 128;\n\n    private static final String NAME_FIRST =\n            \"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz_\";\n    private static final String NAME_REST =\n            \"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789_-\";\n\n    private final String name;\n    private final String path;\n    private final String sameSite;\n\n    /**\n     * @param suppliedName     cookie name; {@code null} or empty selects {@link #DEFAULT_NAME}\n     * @param suppliedPath     cookie path; {@code null} or empty selects {@link #DEFAULT_PATH}\n     * @param suppliedSameSite exactly {@code Strict} or {@code Lax}, any case; {@code null} or\n     *                         empty selects {@link #DEFAULT_SAME_SITE}\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if a value is malformed, or a {@code __Host-} name is paired\n     *         with a path the prefix contract forbids\n     */\n    public static SessionCookiePolicy of(String suppliedName, String suppliedPath, String suppliedSameSite) {\n        String name = orDefault(suppliedName, DEFAULT_NAME);\n        String path = orDefault(suppliedPath, DEFAULT_PATH);\n        String requestedSameSite = orDefault(suppliedSameSite, DEFAULT_SAME_SITE);\n\n        validateName(name);\n        validatePath(path);\n        String canonicalSameSite = canonicalSameSite(requestedSameSite);\n\n        // A __Host- name is a promise the browser enforces by dropping the cookie. Secure and\n        // host-only are unconditional invariants here, so Path is the only one left to check; if\n        // an operator pairs the prefix with a subpath the cookie would never be stored, which is a\n        // broken deployment rather than a policy we should paper over.\n        if (name.startsWith(\"__Host-\") && !\"/\".equals(path)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                    \"cookie name uses the __Host- prefix but path is not '/': refusing to start\");\n        }\n        return new SessionCookiePolicy(name, path, canonicalSameSite);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Falls back only on an absent or zero-length value. A whitespace-only setting is a typo and is\n     * passed through for validation, where it is refused; values are never trimmed into validity.\n     */\n    private static String orDefault(String value, String fallback) {\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            return fallback;\n        }\n        return value;\n    }\n\n    private static void validateName(String name) {\n        if (name.length() > MAX_NAME_LENGTH) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name is longer than \" + MAX_NAME_LENGTH + \" characters\");\n        }\n        if (NAME_FIRST.indexOf(name.charAt(0)) < 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name has an illegal first character\");\n        }\n        for (int i = 1; i < name.length(); i++) {\n            if (NAME_REST.indexOf(name.charAt(i)) < 0) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name has an illegal character\");\n            }\n        }\n        if (RESERVED_NAMES.contains(name.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT))) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie name collides with a protocol keyword\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void validatePath(String path) {\n        if (path.length() > MAX_PATH_LENGTH) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path is longer than \" + MAX_PATH_LENGTH + \" characters\");\n        }\n        if (path.charAt(0) != '/') {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path must start with '/'\");\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < path.length(); i++) {\n            char c = path.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n                    || c == '/' || c == '_' || c == '-' || c == '.';\n            if (!allowed) {\n                // Rejects ';', '\"', ',', ':', CR, LF, NUL, space and every non-ASCII byte, which is\n                // what stops a configured path injecting a cookie attribute or a header line.\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie path has an illegal character\");\n            }\n        }\n        if (path.indexOf(\"//\") >= 0 || path.indexOf(\"/./\") >= 0 || path.indexOf(\"/../\") >= 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                    \"cookie path must be normalized and must not contain '//' or dot segments\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static String canonicalSameSite(String value) {\n        if (\"strict\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n            return \"Strict\";\n        }\n        if (\"lax\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n            return \"Lax\";\n        }\n        if (\"none\".equalsIgnoreCase(value)) {\n            // SameSite=None is what a cross-site embed needs. It turns a first-party session cookie\n            // into a third-party cookie whose only remaining guard is Secure, and modern browsers\n            // increasingly drop it outright. Refused: an operator who really needs cross-site\n            // posting should make that call in code review, not in a properties file.\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"SameSite=None is refused; use Strict or Lax\");\n        }\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"SameSite must be Strict or Lax\");\n    }\n\n    private SessionCookiePolicy(String name, String path, String sameSite) {\n        this.name = name;\n        this.path = path;\n        this.sameSite = sameSite;\n    }\n\n    public String name() {\n        return name;\n    }\n\n    public String path() {\n        return path;\n    }\n\n    public String sameSite() {\n        return sameSite;\n    }\n\n    /** Always true; see the class note on why this is not configuration. */\n    public boolean secure() {\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /** Always true; see the class note on why this is not configuration. */\n    public boolean httpOnly() {\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /** Never set: the cookie is host-only, so no other host can read it or shadow it. */\n    public String domain() {\n        return null;\n    }\n\n    /** {@code 0} means \"browser session only\": no Max-Age/Expires attribute is emitted. */\n    public int maxAge() {\n        return 0;\n    }\n\n    /** Attribute summary only; this type never holds session material. */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"SessionCookiePolicy[name=\" + name + \", path=\" + path + \", sameSite=\" + sameSite\n                + \", secure=true, httpOnly=true, hostOnly=true, sessionCookie=true]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"1f4baa23-738e-440e-a9ea-137c16dae028","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_a810a1e5dd2441ce88ea80db","is_error":false,"content":"package com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n\n/**\n * Emits the session cookie on a servlet response. This is the only class in the component that\n * touches a Servlet API type, so the security decision stays testable without a container.\n *\n * <p>The identifier is re-validated immediately before emission. {@link SessionId} already validated\n * it, but the value crosses a trust boundary at this line: a later maintainer could introduce a path\n * that hands this writer an identifier from somewhere else. The response is therefore only mutated\n * after the second check passes, and the check is reject-only (no normalisation), so a rejected value\n * cannot reach the response at all.\n */\npublic final class SessionCookieWriter {\n\n    private static final String SAME_SITE_ATTRIBUTE = \"SameSite\";\n\n    /** Any response that can carry session material is uncacheable, without exception. */\n    private static final String NO_STORE = \"no-store, max-age=0\";\n\n    private final SessionCookiePolicy policy;\n\n    public SessionCookieWriter(SessionCookiePolicy policy) {\n        if (policy == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie policy is required\");\n        }\n        this.policy = policy;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Writes the anti-caching headers and then the {@code Set-Cookie} header. Nothing else.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code no-store} is set first so that if the cookie write below somehow fails, the partial\n     * response that reaches the network is already uncacheable. Both must precede the first body byte:\n     * a servlet container silently discards headers set afterwards.\n     *\n     * @param sessionIdentifier identifier already accepted by the trusted session owner\n     * @param response          response to mutate\n     * @throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException if the identifier is not a well-formed token; the\n     *         response then carries the cache headers and no cookie\n     */\n    public void writeSessionCookie(SessionId sessionIdentifier, HttpServletResponse response)\n            throws InvalidSessionIdentifierException {\n        if (response == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"response is required\");\n        }\n        // Unconditional and first: a response that carried, failed to carry, or partially carried\n        // session material must never be storable by a browser or an intermediary.\n        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", NO_STORE);\n        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n        if (sessionIdentifier == null) {\n            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(SessionId.Rejection.ABSENT);\n        }\n        String value = sessionIdentifier.value();\n        SessionId.Rejection rejection = SessionId.inspect(value);\n        if (rejection != SessionId.Rejection.NONE) {\n            // The reason class is safe to log. The value is interpolated nowhere below.\n            throw new InvalidSessionIdentifierException(rejection);\n        }\n\n        Cookie cookie = new Cookie(policy.name(), value);\n        cookie.setPath(policy.path());\n        cookie.setHttpOnly(policy.httpOnly());\n        cookie.setSecure(policy.secure());\n        // setMaxAge is deliberately never called. Tomcat renders maxAge == 0 as\n        // \"Expires=Tue Nov 17 10:15:05 GMT 1995\", an already-expired cookie the browser discards, so\n        // a session cookie must keep the API default of emitting no expiry attribute at all.\n        // Servlet 6.0 has no SameSite setter; the attribute map is the supported extension point and\n        // Tomcat 10.1 renders it into Set-Cookie. Host-only by deliberately never calling setDomain.\n        cookie.setAttribute(SAME_SITE_ATTRIBUTE, policy.sameSite());\n        // addCookie, not setHeader: the container owns header syntax and quoting. One call emits one\n        // Set-Cookie, so there is no header-injection surface even if a name were ever wrong.\n        response.addCookie(cookie);\n    }\n\n    /** The policy in force; for startup logging and for the caller's success page. */\n    public SessionCookiePolicy policy() {\n        return policy;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"5ae49827-b3c1-4f78-b302-12133a2ff40b","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_83e423cee59b4fc88874b09c","is_error":false,"content":"package com.acme.portal.session;\n\nimport java.util.ServiceConfigurationError;\nimport java.util.ServiceLoader;\nimport java.util.logging.Level;\nimport java.util.logging.Logger;\n\n/**\n * Discovers the application's {@link SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier} from the classpath.\n *\n * <p>Why a service loader and not a hard dependency: this component must not know where sessions\n * live. It needs one boolean from whoever owns that state, and a compile-time dependency on a\n * datastore would either drag a driver in or force a second HTTP hop with its own timeout, retry and\n * failure semantics to get right.\n *\n * <p>The two risks a loader introduces are both closed here:\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>Ambiguity.</b> More than one provider is a configuration error with an obvious\n *       security consequence -- which store wins? -- so it is refused rather than resolved by\n *       classpath order.</li>\n *   <li><b>Foreign code.</b> Whatever is loaded is the authority on \"is this session valid\", so its\n *       code location is handed to {@link DeploymentHygiene} and a writable location stops the\n *       component from serving at all. The discovery below is deliberately shaped so that check\n *       happens <i>before</i> the candidate is instantiated: a hygiene verdict reached after the\n *       class has run its static initialiser and constructor reports a compromise, it does not\n *       prevent one.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>The interface is declared in this package and implemented by the application; {@code\n * META-INF/services} names the implementation. A provider that throws in its constructor, or cannot\n * be instantiated, yields {@code null} and the servlet refuses every request.\n */\npublic final class SessionStateVerifierProvider {\n\n    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(SessionStateVerifierProvider.class.getName());\n\n    private SessionStateVerifierProvider() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @return the single deployed verifier, or {@code null} if there is none, more than one, or the\n     *         deployed one cannot be constructed. Never returns a substitute that guesses.\n     */\n    public static SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier load() {\n        return load(SessionStateVerifierProvider.class.getClassLoader());\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Same contract, over an explicit loader. Package private on purpose: no caller outside this\n     * package has any business choosing where the verifier is looked for. The seam exists because the\n     * ordering below is invisible in the return value -- a candidate in a writable location yields\n     * {@code null} whether it was refused or merely refused after it had already run -- so the only way\n     * to test that the gate precedes the code is to point the loader at a directory whose mode the test\n     * controls and ask whether the candidate's class initialiser ran.\n     */\n    static SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier load(ClassLoader where) {\n        ServiceLoader<SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier> loader =\n                ServiceLoader.load(SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier.class, where);\n        java.util.List<ServiceLoader.Provider<SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier>> providers =\n                new java.util.ArrayList<ServiceLoader.Provider<SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier>>();\n        try {\n            // stream() rather than iterator(), and it matters: a Provider element hands over its\n            // Class without constructing anything, and the loader defines a candidate class without\n            // initialising it. So the static initialiser and the constructor of a candidate have not\n            // run at the point the hygiene verdict is reached below. With iterator() the old code\n            // called next(), which built the object -- meaning that by the time the servlet asked\n            // where the verifier came from, the code from that location had already executed. The\n            // check belonged to the post-mortem department, and a verifier planted in a writable\n            // directory had already had its turn.\n            loader.stream().forEach(providers::add);\n        } catch (ServiceConfigurationError discoveryFailed) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"the session verifier could not be discovered, exceptionType={0}\",\n                    discoveryFailed.getClass().getName());\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (providers.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (providers.size() > 1) {\n            // Counted without instantiating any of them, so an ambiguous deployment -- which store\n            // wins? -- refuses without executing any candidate's code either.\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"more than one session verifier is deployed; refusing to\"\n                    + \" guess which session store is authoritative\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        ServiceLoader.Provider<SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier> only = providers.get(0);\n        Class<?> candidate;\n        try {\n            candidate = only.type();\n        } catch (ServiceConfigurationError unloadable) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"the deployed session verifier could not be loaded,\"\n                    + \" exceptionType={0}\", unloadable.getClass().getName());\n            return null;\n        }\n        // The gate that the ordering above exists to make meaningful: this class is about to run\n        // code this component does not own, and it is the authority on whether a session exists, so\n        // its code location is proven read-only first. Names no path to the caller, and the reason it\n        // returns names a path and nothing else.\n        String hygieneProblem;\n        try {\n            hygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(candidate);\n        } catch (RuntimeException hygieneCheckFailed) {\n            hygieneProblem = \"the deployment hygiene check failed: \"\n                    + hygieneCheckFailed.getClass().getName();\n        }\n        if (hygieneProblem != null) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"a deployed session verifier was refused before it was\"\n                    + \" instantiated: {0}\", hygieneProblem);\n            return null;\n        }\n        final SessionHandoffService.SessionStateVerifier verifier;\n        try {\n            verifier = only.get();\n        } catch (ServiceConfigurationError brokenProvider) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"a deployed session verifier could not be instantiated,\"\n                    + \" exceptionType={0}\", brokenProvider.getClass().getName());\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (verifier == null) {\n            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"a deployed session verifier factory returned null\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        // Log the loaded class name only. It is a class, not a credential, and an operator needs it to\n        // diagnose \"why is everything refused\".\n        LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"session verifier loaded from {0}\", candidate.getName());\n        return verifier;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"4f0640e8-c7e2-4d78-ad8d-b9799efbaf98","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_4e27e8ae2b8042e2891ec08b","is_error":false,"content":"package com.acme.portal.session;\n\n/**\n * Raised when the identifier supplied by the trusted session owner is not a well-formed opaque\n * token.\n *\n * <p>Checked on purpose: every caller is forced to decide the failure behaviour, and the only\n * correct one is to refuse the handoff. Executive Summary\n\nThis is a static, read-only security review of a Jakarta Servlet 6.0 \"session\nhandoff\" component that turns an assertion set by a trusted in-JVM authenticator\ninto a single hardened `__Host-` session cookie. The reviewed tree is untrusted\ngenerated code; it was read, not executed, built, or imported.\n\nThe component is a notably hardened reference implementation. Across the full\nHTTP decision path it never reads client-controlled session input (no cookie,\nquery, body, header, or `*-For` value), re-verifies every asserted identifier\nagainst a server-side authority, fails closed to HTTP 503 wherever a\ncollaborator is absent/ambiguous/throwing or the deployment hygiene check cannot\nbe completed, uses a constant-work equaliser plus byte-identical refusal bodies\nto suppress timing and response-shape oracles, and encodes every value it emits\ninto HTML. A mutation battery (`tools/mutants.py`) deliberately weakens each\nguard and the harness is written to catch it, which corroborates that the\nguards are live rather than decorative.\n\n**No High or Critical findings were identified.** Two LOW findings are reported:\nan incomplete code-location coverage in the startup hygiene gate under a WAR\ndeployment (CWE-426), and a 48-bit truncated digest used as a comparison value\nthat becomes an offline verification oracle if the *trusted issuer* ever mints\nlow-entropy identifiers (CWE-326). The latter is a residual the source itself\ndocuments as an integration contract. The remaining reviewed controls — cookie\nhardening, header/CRLF-injection resistance, log-secret masking and log forging,\nXSS output encoding, rate limiting, and the `InetAddress` parser surface —\nheld up under the matched skills and are recorded in Section 6.\n\n## 2. Scope\n\n| File | Purpose | Security-relevant behaviour |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffServlet.java` | HTTP adapter / lifecycle | Never calls `getSession`; reads only `getRemoteAddr` + one trusted request attribute; fail-closed `serveNothing()`; constant headers/body shape; GET/HEAD parity (HEAD issues no cookie); 429/501/405 handling; logs only fingerprint + exception type |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffService.java` | Pure decision | Validate → budget → verify order; null verifier = `DENY_ALL`; verifier exception ⇒ invalid; `toString` leaks only the outcome |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionId.java` | Identifier value type | Reject-only `inspect` (no trim/case-fold/normalise); 32–128 base64url charset; `SecureRandom` `generate()`; truncated-SHA-256 `fingerprint()` is the only loggable material |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionCookiePolicy.java` | Cookie description | `secure()`/`httpOnly()` hardcoded true; host-only (`domain()` null); session cookie (`maxAge()` 0); refuses `SameSite=None`; `__Host-` requires `Path=/` |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionCookieWriter.java` | Emission path | Re-validates value at the boundary; sets `no-store` before the cookie; `addCookie` not `setHeader`; never `setMaxAge`/`setDomain` |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffSettings.java` | Config from env | 8 vars, type/length/range validated; rejects non-printable env bytes; blocks reserved Jakarta/Java attribute prefixes; enforces `maxPerWindow ≤ globalMax` |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/AttemptLimiter.java` | Fixed-window throttle | Whole `tryAcquire` under one monitor; per-source + global + tracking-saturation ceilings; global checked before increment; bounded bucket map with O(1) reclaim; monotonic clock; reduced ceiling for unknown sources |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/ClientAddress.java` | Bucket key from socket address | Never reads `*-For`; strict dotted-quad / IPv6-literal grammar enforced **before** `InetAddress.getByName`; folds `::ffff:a.b.c.d`→/32, v6→/64; shared reduced `UNKNOWN` lane |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/CacheSupport.java` | SHA-256 helper | ThreadLocal digest; feeds parts in chunks (no credential copy to prefix); zeroes scratch buffers |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/DeploymentHygiene.java` | Startup code-location gate | Inspects `java.class.path` + named code-owner code sources; refuses on `OTHERS_WRITE`/missing/unreadable/non-POSIX; `logSafe` strips control chars (anti log-forging) |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/ResponseBody.java` | Response bodies | Byte-identical `REFUSAL` for every denial; grants interpolate only the HTML-escaped cookie name; output-width bound on escaping |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/WorkEqualizer.java` | Timing equaliser | Constant SHA-256 work on every path; constant cost independent of inputs; clones shared input |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionStateVerifierProvider.java` | `ServiceLoader` discovery | `stream()`+`type()` so a candidate's static-init/ctor never runs before the hygiene verdict; refuses ambiguous (count>1) and unloaded providers; fail-closed |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/RequestAttributeAssertionSource.java` | Trusted-input reader | Reads only `request.getAttribute(name)`; accepts only `String`; exception ⇒ null |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/TrustedSessionAssertionSource.java` | Contract interface | Forbids reading client data / logging the value / throwing |\n| `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/InvalidSessionIdentifierException.java` | Checked rejection | Message = rejection enum name only; never carries the value; `Locale.ROOT` lowercasing |\n| `deploy/web.xml` | Shipped descriptor | COOKIE tracking mode; `http-only`/`secure` as intent; error/`Throwable` → static `error.html`; test-only marker |\n| `deploy/web.test-only.xml` | Test-only fragment | Injected into TEST wars only; unauthenticated `StubControl` on `/stub/control/*`; **must never ship** (enforced by `build.sh`) |\n| `deploy/WEB-INF/static/error.html` | Static error page | No scriptlet, no request data |\n| `build.sh` | Dev-only build/e2e | Verifies servlet-api jar (published SHA-1 + pinned SHA-512) and Tomcat tarball (pinned SHA-512) before use; stray-byte + XML gates; `-Werror`; asserts packaged WAR class set |\n| `src/test/**` | Harness, e2e client, stubs, probe verifier | Test scaffolding; only `Stub*.class` ship, harness/client/probe excluded (asserted by `build.sh`) |\n| `tools/mutants.py` | Mutation battery | Weakens each guard to prove the harness detects it (attests guards are live) |\n\nOut of scope: `build/apache-tomcat-10.1.60/**`, `*.class`, `*.war`,\n`tools/*.tar.gz`, `tools/*.jar` (vendored build inputs), and the generation\nmeta files at the source root (`prompt.txt`, `prompt_hash.txt`, `result.md`,\n`return.txt`, `README.md`).\n\n## 3. Skills Applied\n\nMatched from the skill library by keyword and applied one at a time to the code:\n\n- **`performing-cryptographic-audit-of-application`** — applied to\n  `SessionId.generate()` / `SECURE_RANDOM` (`SessionId.java:210`, `:269`),\n  `SessionId.fingerprint()` (`SessionId.java:246-249`) and the truncated-digest\n  constant (`SessionId.java:61`), and `CacheSupport` (`CacheSupport.java`).\n  Surfaced **SEC-02**; confirmed `generate()` uses 256 bits of `SecureRandom`\n  and that `CacheSupport` avoids copying the credential to prefix it (§6).\n- **`testing-for-xss-vulnerabilities`** — applied to `ResponseBody.granted()` and\n  `escapeHtml()` (`ResponseBody.java:57-58`, `:67-110`). No XSS: the only\n  interpolated value is the cookie name, whose charset excludes HTML\n  metacharacters, and it is escaped regardless (§6).\n- **`implementing-api-rate-limiting-and-throttling`** — applied to\n  `AttemptLimiter.tryAcquire()` (`AttemptLimiter.java:182-204`). Verified the\n  global lane is checked before charging a source, tracking is bounded, and the\n  reclaim work inside the monitor is O(1) (§6).\n- **`exploiting-server-side-request-forgery`** — applied to\n  `ClientAddress` (`ClientAddress.java:205-222`). Confirmed the grammar is\n  proven before `InetAddress.getByName`, so attacker text never reaches the OS\n  resolver (§6).\n\nNo skill in the library is named specifically for cookie\nhardening (`__Host-` prefix), HTTP response-header / CRLF injection,\nCWE-117 log forging, CWE-426 untrusted search path, or timing side-channels.\nThose were reviewed against general OWASP/CWE practice; SEC-01 (CWE-426)\noriginates from that general review, and the rest are covered in Section 6.\n\n## 4. Summary of Findings\n\n| ID | Title | Severity | CWE | Location |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| SEC-01 | Startup hygiene gate does not cover the webapp classloader's own repositories under a WAR | Low | CWE-426 | `SessionHandoffServlet.java:91`, `:118`; `DeploymentHygiene.java:47-70` |\n| SEC-02 | 48-bit truncated fingerprint is an offline verification oracle for low-entropy identifiers | Low | CWE-326 | `SessionId.java:61`, `:246-249` |\n\nBoth findings are Low. No High/Critical/Medium findings were identified.\n\n## 5. Detailed Findings\n\n### SEC-01 — Hygiene gate misses WEB-INF repositories in a WAR deployment\n\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-426 (Untrusted Search Path) — incomplete enumeration of code\n  locations whose write-accessibility is being attested.\n- **Location:** `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionHandoffServlet.java:91`\n  and `:118` (call sites);\n  `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/DeploymentHygiene.java:47-70`\n  (the enumeration).\n\n**Vulnerable code**\n\n`DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem` builds the set of locations to attest from\ntwo sources only — the JVM `java.class.path`, plus the code source of each\nexplicitly-passed code-owner class:\n\n```java\n// DeploymentHygiene.java:45-70\nstatic String describeProblem(Class<?>... codeOwners) {\n    Set<Path> locations = new LinkedHashSet<Path>();\n    String classpath = System.getProperty(\"java.class.path\", \"\");\n    if (!classpath.isEmpty()) {\n        String[] entries = classpath.split(File.pathSeparator);\n        if (entries.length > MAX_ENTRIES) {\n            return \"the JVM classpath has more than \" + MAX_ENTRIES + \" entries\";\n        }\n        for (String entry : entries) {\n            Path path = toPath(entry);\n            if (path != null) {\n                locations.add(path);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    for (Class<?> codeOwner : codeOwners) {\n        // ...\n        Path fromCodeSource = codeSourceLocation(codeOwner);\n        // ...\n        locations.add(fromCodeSource);\n    }\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\nand the servlet passes exactly two code owners:\n\n```java\n// SessionHandoffServlet.java:91\nhygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class);\n// ...\n// SessionHandoffServlet.java:118\nhygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(SessionHandoffServlet.class, verifier.getClass());\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem**\n\nThe servlet is deployed as a WAR. In that layout `java.class.path` is the\ncontainer bootstrap path (Tomcat's `bootstrap.jar`, `tomcat-juli`, etc.); it\ndoes **not** contain the web application's `WEB-INF/classes` or the jars under\n`WEB-INF/lib`, which are loaded by the webapp classloader. Consequently the only\nwebapp-side bytecode the gate attests is the code source of the two named\nclasses: `SessionHandoffServlet` itself (→ `WEB-INF/classes`) and\n`verifier.getClass()` (the verifier's own jar). The verifier's *transitive*\ndependencies — e.g. the session-store driver it delegates to\n(`sessstore-driver.jar`, a JDBC/Redis/Kafka client) sitting in `WEB-INF/lib` —\nare never inspected for world-writability. An attacker who can write inside the\ndeployed directory tree but who does not (yet) hold write access to the two\nattested locations could plant or replace such a dependency jar and have it\nloaded as part of the \"is this session valid\" authority without the hygiene gate\never objecting. The impact is bounded (the attacker still needs local write\naccess to the deployment, and every security decision still fails closed on a\nnull/throwing verifier), which is why this is Low rather than higher — but it is\na real gap between the gate's intent (\"prove the code we are about to run is\nread-only\") and what it actually enumerates.\n\n**Fix**\n\nEnumerate the webapp classloader's own repositories as well, from the servlet,\nusing the `ServletContext` real paths for the two webapp locations, and pass them\ninto an overload of `describeProblem`. Example:\n\n```java\n// SessionHandoffServlet.java (init), replacing the two describeProblem calls\njava.util.List<java.nio.file.Path> extra = new java.util.ArrayList<>();\nString webInf = getServletContext().getRealPath(\"/WEB-INF\");\nif (webInf != null) {\n    java.nio.file.Path lib = java.nio.file.Paths.get(webInf, \"lib\");\n    if (java.nio.file.Files.isDirectory(lib)) {\n        try (java.util.stream.Stream<java.nio.file.Path> jars = java.nio.file.Files.list(lib)) {\n            jars.forEach(extra::add);            // every WEB-INF/lib/*.jar\n        } catch (java.io.IOException e) {\n            extra.add(lib);                      // let the gate fail closed on unreadable\n        }\n    }\n    extra.add(java.nio.file.Paths.get(webInf, \"classes\"));\n}\n// Pass the webapp repositories + both code owners to a Path-accepting overload\n// that applies the existing worldWritable()/parent/missing-entry checks to each.\nhygieneProblem = DeploymentHygiene.describeProblem(\n        extra, SessionHandoffServlet.class, verifier.getClass());\n```\n\nThe `describeProblem` overload then folds the supplied `extra` paths into the\nsame `locations` set the `for (Path location : locations)` loop already\nattests, preserving the current \"unknown is not safe\" behaviour for each added\nentry.\n\n### SEC-02 — 48-bit fingerprint is an offline oracle when the issuer's entropy is low\n\n- **Severity:** Low (conditional on the trusted issuer's identifier entropy)\n- **CWE:** CWE-326 (Inadequate Encryption Strength / insufficient comparison\n  strength); related to CWE-327 (use of a broken/risky digest as a comparator).\n- **Location:** `src/main/java/com/acme/portal/session/SessionId.java:61` and\n  `:246-249`.\n\n**Vulnerable code**\n\n```java\n// SessionId.java:61\nprivate static final int FINGERPRINT_HEX = 12;   // 48 bits of SHA-256\n```\n\n```java\n// SessionId.java:246-250\npublic String fingerprint() {\n    // Two parts rather than one concatenation: see the note in CacheSupport about not copying a\n    // bearer credential merely to prefix it.\n    return CacheSupport.digestHexPrefix(new String[]{FINGERPRINT_PREFIX, value}, FINGERPRINT_HEX);\n}\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem**\n\n`fingerprint()` is the *only* session material the component ever logs (grant\nlines, and `toString()`/exception masking), so its strength is a real security\nproperty, not a cosmetic one. A 48-bit truncated digest is not a one-way mask —\nit is a **verifier**: anyone who obtains the logs can, entirely offline, confirm\na guessed identifier against a logged line. For identifiers with genuine\nentropy this is safe (2^48 work per line yields no usable value), which is why\nthis is conditional. The exposure is concrete when the trusted upstream issuer\nmints identifiers from a space smaller than the 48 bits the digest publishes —\na short counter, a truncated UUID, a timestamp with a fixed prefix. In that case\na log leak turns into an enumeration oracle over the small identifier space,\nwithout any access to the session store. The component cannot close this in\n`inspect()` (deliberately — guessing about entropy caused false rejections\nbefore), so the burden is stated as a deployment requirement, but a reviewer\nshould still flag the fixed 48-bit comparison width as the residual it is.\n\n**Fix**\n\nWiden the published digest so the offline-confirmation cost is far above the\nlargest identifier space an integrator might plausibly use, and — if a\ndeployment secret is acceptable — make it a keyed MAC so the log is not an\noffline-verifiable function of the value at all:\n\n```java\n// Option A (no new secret): publish 96 bits instead of 48.\nprivate static final int FINGERPRINT_HEX = 24;   // 96 bits\n\n// Option B (stronger, needs a deployment secret): keyed, so the digest is not\n// a public function of the identifier and cannot be verified offline at all.\n// static { KEY = <read once from a secret manager>; }\nprivate static final javax.crypto.Mac MAC =\n        javax.crypto.Mac.getInstance(\"HmacSHA256\");   // per-call, not shared/ThreadLocal-unsafe\n```\n\nIf neither is adopted, keep the current width but treat \"issuer entropy ≥ 128\nbits\" as a **hard pre-acceptance gate** in the integration contract (assert it\nin `SessionStateVerifier` implementations and in deployment review), rather than\nleaving it only as narrative in the Javadoc and README.\n\n## 6. Verified Controls and Checks With No Finding\n\nThe following were reviewed (mostly via the matched skills in Section 3) and\nheld; they are recorded so the coverage is explicit.\n\n**Trusted-input boundary (no client-controlled session input).**\n`SessionHandoffServlet` never calls `request.getSession`, and the assertion is\nread only via `RequestAttributeAssertionSource.readAssertion` →\n`request.getAttribute(name)` (`RequestAttributeAssertionSource.java:39-47`),\nwhich accepts a `String` and returns `null` on any exception. Cookies, query,\nbody, and headers are never consulted for identity. `ClientAddress` never reads\n`X-Forwarded-For` or any `*-For` header (`ClientAddress.java:21`). Choosing the\nsocket peer (`getRemoteAddr`, `SessionHandoffServlet.java:256`) as the rate-limit\nbucket — instead of a spoofable `XFF` — is the security-correct choice for an\nauthorization decision; the source notes the reverse-proxy caveat as an\noperational, not security, tradeoff.\n\n**Reject-only validation / canonicalization.** `SessionId.inspect`\n(`SessionId.java:112-131`) enforces length 32–128 and the base64url charset with\nno trimming, case-folding, or normalisation, so two byte-strings cannot fold to\none session. `LOW_CHAR_DIVERSITY` is applied only to `generate()`'s own output\nvia `selfTest` (`SessionId.java:146`), not to asserted ids — the mutation battery\ndocuments that applying it to the request path false-rejected ~1.7% of\nstore-vouched ids, so this scoping is deliberate, not an oversight.\n\n**Cookie hardening.** `SessionCookiePolicy` hardcodes `secure()`/`httpOnly()` to\n`true` (`SessionCookiePolicy.java` `secure()`/`httpOnly()`), `domain()` to\n`null` (host-only) and `maxAge()` to `0` (session cookie). `canonicalSameSite`\nrefuses `None` and anything other than `Strict`/`Lax`; `__Host-` requires\n`Path=/`; name/path charsets exclude `;`, `\"`, `,`, `:`, CR, LF, NUL, and\nnon-ASCII. `build.sh`'s mutation battery carries `POLICY-*`/`WRITER-*` mutants\nthat prove these invariants are enforced, not aspirational.\n\n**HTTP response-header / CRLF injection.** The cookie is emitted with\n`response.addCookie(...)` (`SessionCookieWriter.java`), so the container owns\nheader syntax and quoting; the value is base64url-only and the name/path are\ncharset-validated; `SessionHandoffSettings` rejects any env byte `<0x21` or\n`>0x7E`, so CR/LF cannot enter a configured name, path, or SameSite.\n\n**Emission-boundary re-validation.** `SessionCookieWriter.writeSessionCookie`\nre-runs `SessionId.inspect(value)` immediately before building the `Cookie` and\nsets `Cache-Control: no-store, max-age=0` / `Pragma: no-cache` *first*, so a\nrejected value never reaches the response and any partial response is already\nuncacheable. It deliberately never calls `setMaxAge`/`setDomain`.\n\n**Fail-closed everywhere.** `serveNothing()` (`SessionHandoffServlet.java:134`)\nnulls all collaborators and sets `serving=false`; init runs it on invalid\nsettings, absent verifier, ambiguous/unloadable provider, or any hygiene problem.\n`SessionHandoffService` maps a null verifier to `DENY_ALL`; a verifier that\nthrows is treated as \"invalid\". The provider uses `ServiceLoader.stream()` +\n`Provider.type()` and calls `describeProblem(candidate)` **before**\n`only.get()` (`SessionStateVerifierProvider.java`), so a verifier planted in a\nwritable location is refused before its static initialiser or constructor runs\n(the mutation battery's `SVC-hygiene-order` mutant pins this ordering).\n`web.xml` maps `Throwable` to a static `error.html`, so even an `Error` yields no\nstack trace to the client.\n\n**Secret leakage / log forging (CWE-117).** The only session material ever logged\nis the fingerprint; grant lines log `fingerprint` only, and errors log\n`exceptionType` (`SessionHandoffServlet.handle` catch at\n`SessionHandoffServlet.java:289`). `InvalidSessionIdentifierException` carries\nonly the rejection enum name (with `Locale.ROOT`), never the value.\n`SessionId`/`SessionHandoffService` `toString()` deliberately expose only the\nfingerprint/outcome. `DeploymentHygiene.logSafe`\n(`DeploymentHygiene.java:101`) strips CR/LF/NUL/DEL from paths so a filename\ncannot forge a second log line.\n\n**Timing / shape oracles.** `WorkEqualizer.apply()` runs constant SHA-256 work\non every path (`SessionHandoffServlet.java:243`), before the serving check and\nbefore the limiter, so response timing cannot rank outcomes; `ResponseBody.REFUSAL`\nis byte-identical for every denial (`ResponseBody.java:19`), and GET/HEAD share\ndecision, headers, and work while HEAD writes no cookie (`writeBody=false`,\n`SessionHandoffServlet.java:232`, `:319`). (Fixed CPU before shedding is an\naccepted tradeoff: the limiter sheds the store round-trip and cookie write, not\nthe equaliser's fixed cost; the source reasons about this and ships a timing\nharness.)\n\n**XSS.** `ResponseBody.granted` interpolates only `escapeHtml(policy.name())`\n(`ResponseBody.java:57-58`, `:67-110`). The cookie-name charset contains no HTML\nmetacharacters, escaping covers `& < > \" '`, control characters become spaces,\nand the output-width bound (`MAX_ESCAPED_LENGTH` charged against `WIDEST_ENTITY`)\nmakes entities whole-or-absent rather than truncated-mid-entity.\n\n**Rate limiting (CWE-799-adjacent resource control).** `AttemptLimiter.tryAcquire`\nis fully under one monitor (`AttemptLimiter.java:182`); the global ceiling is\nchecked *before* charging either counter (`:198-204`) so a shared-lane flood\ncannot spend innocent sources' allowance; tracking is bounded\n(`maxTrackedSources`) with an O(1) `RECLAIM_CANDIDATES`-sized reclaim hand so one\nstalled client's scan stays independent of crowd size; the clock is monotonic;\nunknown sources get a reduced ceiling.\n\n**SSRF / resolver reach (CWE-404-adjacent).** `ClientAddress.parse` applies a\ncharacter allowlist and full dotted-quad / IPv6-literal grammar\n(`ClientAddress.java:41`, `:131`, `:205-212`) **before** calling\n`InetAddress.getByName` (`:222`), so inputs like `517996801`, `0x7f.1`, or a\nhostname never reach the OS resolver; `::ffff:a.b.c.d` folds to /32 while the\nobsoleted `::a.b.c.d` form deliberately does not fold.\n\n**Build/provenance (dev tooling).** `build.sh` verifies the servlet-api jar\nagainst its published SHA-1 **and** a pinned SHA-512, and the Tomcat tarball\nagainst a pinned SHA-512, before either is used/extracted; gates stray non-ASCII\nbytes and malformed `web.xml`; compiles `-Werror`; and asserts the packaged WAR\ncarries the four test `Stub` classes and **not** `Harness`/`E2EClient`/\n`ProbeVerifier`. The unauthenticated `StubControlServlet` on `/stub/control/*`\nexists only in `web.test-only.xml`, which is injected solely into throwaway test\nwars and is not part of the shipped `deploy/web.xml`.\n"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":160504,"output_tokens":8839,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":169343}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"2a720949-9623-4b9d-a022-b08eb19cce0b","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_a1174f3a62704851a7552359","is_error":false,"content":"Successfully created and wrote to new file: /data/results-cyber/qwen/qwen/set_a/post_research/1/security/security_review.md."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"8c4ad6d8-dc89-42a1-b6bc-455b42e3378c","session_id":"3ceee8d5-a389-4f41-a946-135c937fdb0a","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"8c4ad6d8-dc89-42a1-b6bc-455b42e3378c","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The deliverable has been written.
